# Open issues

The running register of every known open issue, moved here from `notes/ISSUES.md` "Open now" on 2026-11-08 (ISSUES B25,
D147) so every side chat and machine can read it. Same lines, same ids. New issues go here, one line each, newest group
first; a fix moves the line to its dated section in `notes/ISSUES.md` with the commit. `notes/ISSUES.md` keeps the
dated history (symptom, cause, fix, guard).

## Open now (the running register; the user, 2026-10-31: "keep a running log of all these known issues")

Every known open issue, one line each, newest group first; a fix moves it to its dated section below with the commit.
Status words: **flagged** (seen, not analysed), **analysed** (cause known, no fix), **proposed** (design written, waiting
on the user), **built, unseen** (code in, not yet seen in a live round).
Added 11-07: **built, not exercised (checked 11-07)** (code in, no round on record has reached it) and **ran, no measure on
record** (the code certainly ran, nobody counted what it did). "Open now" is rolled up by area on the roadmap (docs/ROADMAP.md, the long view, every open line placed) and re-read whenever the queue is re-cut (docs/QUEUE.md, the week).
**Numbering from 2026-11-08 evening:** the side chat numbers its new lines from B200 upward and the build chat keeps counting from B143, so two chats writing the same hour never clash and a merge never renumbers (today's merges renumbered twice; B130 to B142 carry their old numbers in brackets). Ids: where two groups used the same id, the older carries a date suffix (W1-1031, R1-1031 to R5-1031, E1-1102, E2-1102,
N6-1031, N7-1031, A1-1031, A2-1031, C1-1101); a bare id is the newer item.

**Added 2026-11-09 night, part twelve (all that is unfinished from Lilith's game; the side chat's lines B242 to B246, B261, built in the build chat; the build chat's own new lines from B262). Evidence: her save 25a51df64905, read from a copy.**

- B242 *built: not everyone speaks every turn, and a beat told again is caught by the engine.* The fantasy's party line said "Give each of them something in the telling" and Sonnet gave five people a paragraph each on Reed Island (turn 28). It now reads "Those the moment touches speak or act, true to what they want and how they are; the rest are there and need nothing, or a word. Not everyone every turn." (the only game with a party line). K3 traced: on turns 11 and 12 the referee's raw time-pass content holds no ruling row at all, though its earlier-turns block carried turn 11's "Maud's broom thumping after" and turn 12 said "Maud's broom thumps after it." The half that failed is the referee's: it never raised repeated_beat, so the engine's quote check never saw one. A reworded rule (the same bit of business given to someone in the background again, even in a few words, even while still going on; a sentence shorter than six words quoted whole) was sent again on turns 11 and 12 on Sonnet and still raised nothing, so the wording stays as a help and the fix is the engine's: repetition.beat_candidates finds a sentence sharing a run of three content words, one of them a person's name, with a sentence of the last three turns; beat_group joins those against the same turn; Engine._beat_check asks the referee's model one question (why first, then repeated; "if in doubt, false"); a yes is a serious repeated_beat ruling by the engine quoting this turn's sentences, a `beat_check` ledger row either way. On her 28 turns three turns have a candidate (6-7 Old Hesk's cackle, 7-9 Hesk by the fire, 11-12 the whistle and the broom); Sonnet said repeated for 12 only. The grimdark saves have two or three each (Hesk's breath, Maud's hum, Tam's small wet sound): one small call on about one turn in ten.

- B243 *built: the People cards show what was learned, and stay true.* A card's notes were the person's `facts`, seeded from the content and shown from the first meeting (Hazel's "Sells pelts at the Market of Hooks; sleeps at the Lantern", Tansy's "knows which alley the Night Market was in last"). Now (a) a content fact shows clause by clause, and only once a telling since the meeting says it: two of its content words in one sentence with the person named in that sentence or the one before (the plain two-words-anywhere test showed Hazel's "sleeps at the Lantern" on turn 28 from "Mirren does not sleep" and a boat's lantern, and Tansy's alley on turn 2 from Maud's "Last night I stood..."); facts learned in play show at once, newest first; (b) the mood shows beside the attitude (Gorm: wary, grateful); (c) `learned <id> value=<fact>, note=replaces <words of the old one>` puts the new fact in the old one's place, matched by two of its words, in the card and in the narrator's list alike (a stale fact there is the contradiction the card fix is for); the Journal command's PEOPLE lines take the card's first note. The narrator's "Known about them" still has every fact, told or not. Her turn 28 replayed: Hazel's card shows nothing (her trade and her bed at the Lantern were never told; the prose only showed a wolf-pelt coat), Tansy's nothing, Maud's nothing (her inn and the paid room were never said in one sentence naming her), Tam's the learned "Wren is alive and well on the island..." only, Gorm's his two learned facts. Seeds are read from the content by id (scenario and hook casts), so old saves need nothing; `shown` starts empty on a save made before, so her content facts stay hidden until a telling says them again.

- B244 *built: what the game master says in an aside is canon, and the game master answers.* The owner, 11-09 night: "I literally asked a girls age once and it was like you can't tell." The ask's prompt now has the game master decide what can plainly be seen if the story has not said it (how old someone looks, said in years; what they wear and carry; what the room holds; where the player is and how they got there), say it as fact, true from then on, and keep "cannot tell" for what is hidden (behind a door, in someone's head, too far or dark, something nobody has told them). The ask's state block carries each person here with the record's description after the role (fantasy hook people_records; named once the name is known). A leaked hidden term is masked (B220's mask_term), not the answer thrown away. After each aside the referee's own prompt runs once more with the aside as the narration and a pass that asks only for learned rows (ASIDE_PART); they land as any learned row does, a ledger row `aside_learned` either way; a row whose words are four in five already in the state or the last three tellings is dropped as stale; who is with the player is left as it is (a first draft passed an empty present and would have emptied the boat). It runs as a task after the answer; a turn waits for it. Replayed: the three grimdark turn-9 states on Gemma: "about sixteen years old ... a stained linen apron over a coarse wool dress", a learned row on the kitchen girl (the first wording gave "a young woman ... cannot tell her exact age"; an example "about sixteen" was copied word for word, so the line says "say it in years"); f2c12e51c4a0 turn 29: "the shingle landing of Reed Island ... arrived here by skiff with Tam Reedy", Elowen's name masked; Lilith's turn 5 "what's the name of the thing in the cellar?" (Sonnet named Gorm) comes back masked; her turn 15 (Sonnet): a learned row for the pale shape; her "where am I currently" (turn 0): no row (Sonnet's row was all in the opening, dropped as stale). The two transcript rows still reach no model.

- B245 *built: the clock in a tense scene.* The owner: "should be able to go as low as 1 during a tense scene like the sirens' ultimatum." Sonnet wrote tempo slow on all 28 of Lilith's turns, so the one-minute floor was never reachable. Engine: tense_why reads the pressing signs (a fight; the game's own: a peril, a compulsion, someone hostile here; the assistant's open_to_callers false; a check this turn or last at a person here; a question to the player waiting; not a move, the player's own room or someone having spoken); in a tense turn the slow floor and the kind-of-turn row's floor stand down (the row's ceiling holds), floor one, with a note. The assistant's tempo line: fast for one beat inside a fight, a chase or a standoff (a threat answered, a thing said under a raised weapon, a whisper while something watches), seconds to a minute. people_here now counts the player's with_them wherever they are filed (her turns 15 to 23: in the skiff with Tam and Hazel, both filed at the Reedway, so "here" was empty for quiet_why too). Her 28 turns: on the stored readings 2 move (3 and 9, 5 to 3 minutes: a check at Maud, at Gorm, the turn before); every turn's reading resent on Sonnet with the new line: fast on 5 turns (16 to 19, 21), open_to_callers false on most of the lake, and 8 turns move (6, 13, 16, 17, 18, 19, 21, 23), 31 minutes in all, from 5 or 10 to 1 to 3 each (turn 11 also moved, by the new reading's own estimate, not by this). Turn 20 could not be resent (its first reading went with the retelling); its neighbours in the standoff, 19 and 21, came back fast.
- B262 *flagged (the build chat, 11-09 night, from B245's replay): the people the player is with keep a stale location.* Tam and Hazel stayed filed at the Reedway through turns 15 to 23 while the player was on the river Marrow and Reed Island with them; people_here now reads with_them, but the Places and People cards ("last seen") and anything else reading a companion's location still see the Reedway. A move of the player could carry with_them along (the engine knows who is with them).

- B246 *built: a thing is held once, and a gift leaves the pockets.* Lilith's turn 8: the referee's player pass and world pass both wrote "new brass whistle"; the first moved Gorm's whistle into her pockets, the second found the id taken and made brass_whistle_10. (a) A new thing for the pockets whose name matches one already carried (one of it, no count) is that thing: nothing added, a note. A listed thing by that name anywhere is still moved, as before (a first draft held it to things within reach and missed Gorm, whose presence lands after the rows). (b) The player's own words handing a thing in the pockets to someone here by name ("handing the whistle to Tansy", "I give Tansy the whistle"; their acts, not their quoted speech) put it in that person's hands when the rows took it from the pockets or did nothing with it, as long as the telling touched it; a note on the turn ("A brass whistle given to Tansy Fell"); the fantasy's after_rows hook, given the state before and after the rows. Replayed against her stored states with the referee's own rows: turn 8 leaves one whistle, in her pockets; turn 10 leaves it with Tansy (before: zeroed, out of the world, the duplicate on her page eighteen turns).
- B224.23 *content, and correct as it stands.* The engine already closes a hook the turn an ending flag is set (tested: wren_home closes the Ferryman's Daughter that turn). wren_found is not one of its endings (done: wren_home, wren_stays, wren_taken_by_force, wren_sold); it is the finding, and in the Barbarian round 5528fb9033ac it was never even set (the rows wrote "Wren found" into a thread note). B259's quest check, replayed on Sonnet for that round's turns 22 to 24, is asked each turn and answers none each time: Wren comes home only once the bell is back. Nothing to change unless the owner wants finding her to end the job.
- B224.24 *built: a thread's giver is who gave it.* The referee's who on a thread was the person it is with ("Hazel hired for the lake", who=hazel), shown as "from Hazel Oakhew" though Kragga hired her. The referee's thread line: who is whoever gave the job or made the ask or the promise to the player, or player when it is the player's own (the person hired named in value); the Quests panel shows no giver for the player's own.

- B261 *built: the Timeline and the Journal.* Lilith's turn 28: 26 journal lines and 32 log lines in the side column, which on a phone ran 4,942px under the story (the Journal alone 1,411px). (a) The game's log (a LogPanel marked timeline: the fantasy's, noir's Case log, the station's Logfile) leaves the columns for a Timeline overlay in the page, opened by a timeline button in the game's header and a Timeline chip under the input: the whole log by day, today open, earlier days folded, read-only; a tap outside, close or Escape shuts it. (b) The journal (a ListPanel marked journal: the fantasy's Journal, noir's Notebook, the station's Findings) is sent whole, newest first, and shows six with "See more (n older)" unfolding in place. (c) The referee's log_line says what happened this turn and that what was learned is a learned player row; each game's learned player line says "may need again ... not what happened this turn (that is the log line)"; the engine (journal_not_log, by each game's journal_path) drops a journal line the rows added that shares two content words beyond the names with the turn's log line and at least half its own (the directive's bare two-word test dropped 21 of her 26 lines, the two-gold offer, the Temple that raises the dead and the lamplighters among them; with the half, 3: turns 3, 21 and 22, each its log line again; turn 8's nest line keeps its forty paces). (d) The Journal and Notebook commands open the overlay on the journal tab instead of printing into the story. At 375px after: the side column 3,785px, the Journal 453px, no sideways scroll; the overlay fits the width. The clown's logs are its own panels and untouched. Item 7 of the player-UI order, done ahead of 1 to 6: the header's ⋯ menu does not exist yet, so the button sits in the header row.

**Added 2026-11-09 night, part fourteen (the owner: an introduction at turn 0).**

- B263 *built: an introduction at turn 0 for each game.* The owner: "some kind of introductory paragraph... the world and your character's state within it"; Lilith: nothing told her who she was, where she was or what was going on. Each scenario has an `introduction` (the owner's texts, verbatim; noir kessler-girl, the fantasy marrowgate, the station alien-infection, in notes/content), shown as the first narrator text at turn 0, a blank line, then the opening_text, one transcript row (gamekit.with_introduction, each game's opening()), so the narrator's window, the summarizer and the save card carry it as the first telling. The fantasy fills {name}, {race}, {klass} as its prefix does ("an elf rogue"). The fantasy lint fails an introduction naming a secret term (gamekit.introduction_secrets), and the tests check all three scenarios' terms; the lint's placeholder rule lets the introduction use {race} and {klass}. Existing saves keep their turn 0; her server takes it at its next move; the grimdark game is the fantasy with a mod and inherits it. The three game pages (Lowtown, Station AI, Marrowgate) show turn 0 as a new game opens; the station page is back on its named instance 2004e52394dc.

**Added 2026-11-09 night, part fifteen (combat, and the Paladin on it; the combat record notes/DESIGN-combat-2026-11-09.md and the magic record §8; numbered from B264, B263 being part fourteen's).**

- B264 *built (§1): the blocks.* A `combat` block on a person (state.CombatBlock: ac, hp and hp_max, attack, damage dice, saves, kind, weight, actions, dc, a band's count and hps, band, fixed, regen, fanatic, lead); the library of SRD 5.1 blocks (games/fantasy/combat.py BLOCKS: commoner, watchman, brute, hunter, captain, skeleton, zombie, ghost, wight, siren, troll, beast) and the defaults by role words for anyone with none (commoner AC 10 hp 4 +2 1d4; Watchman or hired blade AC 12 hp 9 +3 1d6+1; brute AC 14 hp 30 +5 1d8+3); a saved block's defaults never undo the library's (fixed, fanatic). The old `moves` read as actions; lint_combat (advisory) names the records still using them and the fighters with no block. The player's block from the sheet: AC as built, attack = the stat (str; dex for ranged; the better for finesse or thrown) + proficiency (+2 at 1 to 4, +3 at 5), damage the SRD weapon's dice + the stat, saves the stat + proficiency in the class's two (a level-1 human paladin: longsword +5, 1d8+3, AC 16). Weights from the library or derived from the numbers. Hit point words for everyone on the player's tiers, a band's in one line ("one destroyed, one bloodied, two unhurt"); a hurt person's word in the state block, the party line and a card badge. The referee's new person whose note says "fights" gets a default block and joins the fight.

- B265 *built (§2): the rolls.* engine/mechanics.py beside the check: attack_roll (d20 + bonus + modifiers against AC; a natural 20 hits and doubles the dice, a natural 1 misses; no tier words), roll_dice ("1d8+3", a crit doubling the dice, never below 0), save_roll (d20 + the stat's save bonus against a DC: a check under another name). The dice line's three forms (combat.attack_line, enemy_line, save_line: "Longsword at the skeleton: 17 against AC 13, hit; 7 damage, the skeleton is bloodied."; "The skeleton's blade: 9 against AC 16, miss."; "Wisdom save against the siren's song: 14 against 13, holds."). A "step lighter" on the player is a flat reduction from one table (STEP_DICE: a blow of 6 or more loses 3, of 3 to 5 loses 2, smaller ones 1), so Rage, the shield and Uncanny Dodge keep working on dice. The check stays for everything that is not a blow with a weapon; a spell attack and a spell save are these two with the casting stat.

- B266 *built (§3 to §6): combat mode, more than one blow, allies, the prompts* (one commit: the mode, the blows and the prompts are one path; a commit without the reading's attack and the narrator's calls could not run a round). state.combat (round, since_turn, allies, target, orders, surprise, defeated, the round's plan); entered when the reading's attack names someone (a target the reading names is taken to be here, B262's stale places) or an <<ENEMY>> call joins; THIS TURN opens "A FIGHT, round N" with the round list (the player's attack, each fighting ally's, each enemy's action per body, the calls to write, everyone's state in words); <<ATTACK id>>, <<ALLY id>> and <<ENEMY id action>> roll when the narrator reaches them; the backstop rolls every listed action the prose left out, the player's own attack included; combat.land puts the hits on everyone (enemies dead or destroyed at 0, down if the words spared them; allies down, dead at -10; morale: a person at a quarter fails a wis save against 10 + level and yields), regenerates a troll unless burned, and ends the fight with XP = the weights x 25. A free turn (no attack, no check, no time) holds everyone; after two in a row the grace ends. Cleave-through, a sweep at two or three (-2 after the first), Extra Attack at level 5, riders (Smite from the class file, Sneak Attack from hiding or beside an ally; an unknown rider noted and ignored), a bow spends arrows, a thrown weapon lies where it fell, bands acting body by body. Allies who fight are the party members with a block of their own or loyal (a bystander takes no part); an order holds from the next turn. The reading's attack {targets, weapon, riders, ally_orders} on the fantasy's own schemas (noir and the station unchanged), the assistant's rule, the narrator's A FIGHT rule (in the content prefix), the protocol line, the referee's: no status rows for anyone the round touched (refused by the engine too). The old moves read as actions (their damage steps as dice, their bonus as the to-hit).
- B267 *built (§7): the Fight panel.* Main column, pinned: the round; everyone with a meter and the number (the player, allies, enemies alike, a band body by body); ailments and buffs beside each (bleeding, blessed, turned, held; never a kind word); the last action in a word; Flee (each enemy within reach one free blow, rolled and landed now) and Yield (people take it, the dead, beasts and fanatics do not) as chips. Attack, damage and save lines show as dice cards in the prose.
- B268 *built (§8, §9): scaling, the simulator, the replays.* A weight on every block; budget = (level + 1) x (1 + fighting party x 0.6) x band x difficulty (0.75 / 1 / 1.25); bands tuned by tools/fight_sim.py to easy 1, fair 2, hard 3, deadly 3.75 (the record's 0.5 / 1 / 1.5 / 2.5 bought one skeleton for both fair and hard at level 1); the count up to six, a remainder of half a body as attack and DCs +1, three quarters as hit points +25%; fixed blocks keep their numbers; each step in the mechanics line. Difficulty also moves enemy attacks and DCs -2 / 0 / +2 and the player's attack the other way. The simulator's table and the two replays are in the report.
- B269 *built (§10, §11): features as data, the Paladin.* The class file's `feature:` (heal, rider, turn, buff; games/fantasy/features.py) as chips and words doors; Rage, Second Wind and the smoke pellets run through it; the Paladin full: Lay on Hands (a pool of 5, 15 at level 3, by touch, bringing an ally up), Divine Smite (holy power, then the slots at level 2; 2d8, 3d8 on the undead; spent only on a hit), Turn Undead (level 1, twice at 3), Defence (a step lighter in mail), at level 2 two slots with Bless (+1 on every roll, ten minutes), Shield of Faith (a step lighter, ten minutes), Cure Wounds (1d8 + cha by touch); Extra Attack at 5. No choices at level up. Conditions may carry stat all.
- B270 *built (§12): the undead and the first skeleton quest* (content, notes/content). Blocks in the library (skeleton, zombie, ghost, wight); the drowned at the Mire's chapels (a zombie band, a grab that drowns), the ghost on Monastery Isle's steps, the wight fixed in the barrow; the_quiet_dead: Wick the Temple's sexton at the Lantern, the catacomb dead (a skeleton band, hard at the player's level) through the broken wall behind Maud's cellar, Ludo Fenn the robber hiding with a river-king's ring (spare him or not), the second way in by the Temple's crypt stair; the deck draws it first (draw_first), since dealing it at the start filled the deck's cap and crowded the first evening. Blocks for who fights today: the sirens, Rook (captain), Gorm (the troll, fixed, regenerating), Hazel (hunter), Tam (commoner). The newcomer check no longer takes "the" or a title for a name ("the drowned of the Mire chapels" held every arrival).
- B272 *built: the attack as flat fields.* Sonnet's API refused the fantasy reading at 2cd5cab: "The compiled grammar is too large, which would cause performance issues" (every fantasy turn on Sonnet failed; caught by the round's first turn; :8016 was never on it). The staked reading was already near the strict grammar's limit: measured on the API, `attack_at` (a list) and `weapon` (a string) fit, a third list (riders, or ally orders) does not. So the intent carries `attack_at` and `weapon` ("longsword, smite": the riders after a comma), read back as one attack by a property; the allies' orders are read by the engine from the player's own words (an ally's name, then an enemy's, in one sentence). Gemma on llama.cpp: the same schema writes the attack (its first try also wrote a str check beside it and left the named smite out).
- B273 *built: the round list reaches the narrator.* The first Sonnet Paladin round (6f607706727b, the_quiet_dead) had 2 narrator ATTACK calls against 20 backstops: THIS TURN never carried "A FIGHT, round N" because the game's standing lines (turn_directives) are read before world_turn makes the plan, so the list was always empty at that point. The list now rides on world_turn's own directives, as the perils' do; turn_directives stays quiet in a fight. Also from that round: the enemies stayed behind when the player moved, so the fight ended by walking (they now follow unless turned), and an armed Divine Smite never showed on the narrator's round line ("Divine Smite ready, on the blow if it lands"). Still open: the backstop's dice reach the mechanics row, not the prose; a turned undead leaves the fight, and chasing it does not start one.
- B274 *built: from the second Paladin round (09196b219219, Sonnet, after B273; narrator calls 15 against 7 backstops, the first round's 2 against 20).* (1) "divine smite" in the reading was ignored as "not something Aldric has" and did not arm the smite (T10); a later armed chip did fire, under the same false note (T12): a rider named by any of its riders, words, name or id is the feature now. (2) The possessed keeper, freed in the prose and friendly in the rows by T13, kept swinging for three turns and Pip kept hitting him; the fight and its XP waited until his morale broke at T15: an enemy whose attitude rises to friendly after joining stands down (counted as won). (3) A blow the backstop lands after the prose showed only "1 hit points (after the prose)"; its dice line is the row now. Still open from that round: a narrator-invented undead (the "pale figure", T7 to T9) is no person, so Turn Undead found nothing to turn; the reading's attack on it named the_step_ghost, a content ghost filed on Monastery Isle, and the attack moved it to the Catacombs; Pip did not obey "stay with him" (T13, T14); a failed subdue check in a fight still costs hit points by its tier (the record's rule) beside the enemies' dice; the lantern-flame refrain T5 to T19 (the read).
- B275 *built (the owner, 11-09): Tansy takes Pip's place.* The owner took Tansy (D141's hedge-witch's apprentice) for a rename of Pip; it was an addition, and Pip's tag-along happening kept bringing Pip into the owner's grimdark game while Lilith met Tansy. Pip leaves the city file: Tansy gets the tag-along (tansy_tags_along), Pip's fence secret and tell (tansy_fence: she sells to Nan for the Guild), Nan's lines and the Guild hook's wants and rewards name her. A save carries its own copy of the cast and the happenings, so the change does not reach a game in progress: notes/scripts/swap_pip_for_tansy.py writes it into a save's snapshots with an owner_fix row; done for the owner's grimdark Ungdall f083ca6fc352 (Pip unmet there; store backed up first, data/backups/textadventure.before-pip-to-tansy.20261109T202228Z.sqlite3). Lilith's save 25a51df64905: Pip removed on the owner's word ("she already has tansy, just remove pip if he never showed up anyway"; he was only ever in the narrator's state listing, turns 1 to 12), his happening with him, by the script's --remove-only, store backed up first (her data folder's backups/textadventure.before-remove-pip.20261109T203125Z.sqlite3), an owner_fix row at turn 28; her pinned content copy still has Pip's row and Nan's lines naming him (read at load) until the owner moves her server.
- B276 *built (the owner, 11-09): Sneak Attack by the round, and the simulator with an ally.* Sneak Attack was "while an ally fights them" whenever any ally was along, whoever they fought; it is now the round's: an enemy who goes for one of the player's allies this round (the owner: "automatic sneak attack bonuses on enemies targeting their allies"), or an enemy an ally is fighting this round (the SRD's ally beside the target), or from hiding. tools/fight_sim.py takes `--ally hazel` (repeatable; a city person with a block, counted in the budget) and reports how often an ally went down; the standard table has Rogue + Hazel and Paladin + Hazel rows. Level 1, Normal, 1000 fights: Rogue alone fair 45%, hard 13%; Rogue + Hazel fair 67%, hard 24%, deadly 5% (Hazel down in 6 to 16% of fights); Sneak Attack rode 854 of the Rogue's 900 hits with Hazel along, none alone. *Flagged, the owner's call:* an ally makes the Paladin's fights worse, not better (Paladin + Hazel fair 92%, hard 43% against 97% and 68% alone; Hazel down in 28% and 64%): the budget's 0.6 a fighting ally buys a whole extra body (two skeletons become three at fair, three become four at hard), more than Hazel adds; the Rogue still sits under the targets with her (B271).
- B277 *built (the owner, 11-09): the narrator is asked never to state the clock.* The owner's grimdark Ungdall (f083ca6fc352) was retold for the clock in the prose five times in 31 turns (22, 23, 26, 27, 31: "The clock reads 20:30.", "The clock in the hall strikes 06:20."); its stored narrations hold no times (each was retold), so the invitation was the prefix's own "if you mention the hour, use that one. Bells ring it." The fantasy prefix's Time rule now reads: never state the clock (or the log's times), in narration or speech, in figures or words; show where the day stands true to the clock (light or dark, the street, a fire burned low) when time has moved or the player asks, not every turn. Read first by a fresh Opus reader (its wording taken, which also closes "twenty past six" and NPC speech). A game's prefix is frozen at creation: the owner's save got the same sentence by an owner_fix (the old text in the row); Lilith's save and the other old saves keep the old line. The clock_in_prose retell stays as the backstop. Watch: the quoted "not long till dawn" may be copied; the state block and the log still carry HH:MM. Noir's prefix has the same "If you mention the hour, use that one" (no clock rule there).
- B278 *built (the owner, 11-09: "give the rogue by default a better weapon and more health, maybe a little better stats"): the Rogue's numbers.* A weapon's make is read from its name (combat.QUALITY: fine, masterwork, dwarf-forged, elven: +1 to the attack and the damage), for any weapon the player carries; the Rogue's two kit blades are a fine rapier and a fine shortsword. Her hit points are the Paladin's (10 at first, 6 a level; were 8 and 5); DEX 4 and CON 2 (were 3 and 1); her Sneak Attack line on the sheet says the round's rule (B276). A level-1 Human Rogue: 12 hit points, AC 15, +7 to hit, 1d8+5 (was 9, 14, +5, 1d8+3); an Elf or Halfling Rogue starts at DEX 5. fight_sim, Normal, 1000 fights: alone fair 84%, hard 49%, deadly 29% (were 45, 13, 4); with Hazel fair 89%, hard 57%, deadly 21%. The Paladin is fair 97%, hard 68%, deadly 50%: the Rogue is now near the targets at fair, still under them at hard and deadly (B271 stays open for the owner). Content only reaches new games: a Rogue already made keeps her sheet.

Trust: pinned (never a body added or removed; an open count filled), named (match_entry, else the kind the words say at home here, else a commoner with a note), located (the city file's new `regions`: the entries at home there weighted 3, anywhere 1; no uniques). Shortlist by the DMG (thresholds per level and member, the multiplier stepped up under three, a lieutenant at three or more, six bodies, two entries); ranked by the engine's own fight code (encounter.sim_fight, which tools/fight_sim.py now uses) against the party at full strength at weak/standard/veteran; then the knob: a body more or fewer while the best is twelve points off. About 1.2 ms a fight; 360 to 630 fights, 0.4 to 0.8 s an encounter at thirty a cell. combat.scale is the generator for a content band (ten fights a cell, on the turn; a count kept, a variant chosen). Enemy moves (the owner, 11-10: "the most rudimentary ai"): a weighted draw among those that make sense (a strike 3, a drain 2, the rest 1, or the move's own weight; no grab on the held, no song on the enthralled); the bestiary simplified (the owner: "our bestiary only needs a very simplified version"). fight_sim's standard table at 300 fights, Normal, level 1: paladin vs 2/3/4 fixed skeletons 96/71/19; the paladin's band easy/normal/hard 82/71/52; rogue vs the six guards 0; rogue's band 99/61/39; with Hazel 94/62/49; Gorm 12 (paladin), 3 (rogue).
- B296 *built (part sixteen §3).* The generator off the turn: FantasyState.encounters (place -> key, one ranked fight per band trivial/easy/normal/hard, fights, seconds) and FantasyState.offer (§4). After a turn the pipeline starts the game's `warm` in a thread when `warm_key` (level | the party's ids | region) is not already cached for the player's place; the next turn's start takes a finished result (`take_warm`, only if the key still holds) and writes an `encounter_cache` ledger row (place, key, seconds, fights, each band's reason); a job still running is left (that turn's fight is a cold miss). `encounter_for(s, band, words, pinned, offset)`: a cache hit when nothing is pinned or named and the key holds, else the generator inline at ten fights a cell, said in the reason. A long rest drops every place's cache but the one rested in. The call report's head counts the draws, their mean seconds and the hit rate; a draw and a choice show at their turns. TA_WARM=off switches the job off (the suite pins it off; the pipeline hand-off has its own test). A draw of four bands for a level-1 paladin: about 1,400 to 2,000 fights, 1.6 to 2.4 s in the thread.

(plus, for impossible, that it cannot be won and the way out is to run or yield) from the cache; state.offer keeps the ids for the place; a narrator's <<ENEMY robber_2>> writes that one in (no other spawn: the call must name an offered id). (b) The referee's resolver (_new_person): an offered id or entry is the offer's; else a person whose words name a monster, or whose row says fights, gets the entry's block (a plural name a band the generator sizes) and joins the fight; a monster takes its entry's name as `called` and attitude -2 at most. (c) A blow at someone the state lacks (world: the reading's attack_at words) goes through bring_in before the round is planned: 'kneeling man in grey hood' a bandit in the fight, 'the Hill men' two to six bandits, 'a pale figure' a specter, each a w.rows encounter row. (d) Content keeps block, count, band, fixed.
