- B302 *built (the owner's rule: every model-facing line read fresh; a subagent read the five new lines as the model on its worst turn).* Its findings, adopted: the offer's "name them" and the narrator's "use their names ... their numbers" could put robber_1 or a hit point in the prose; the referee's rule never said "attacking" (a sneering barkeep filed as an enemy); "cannot be beaten" risked telling the player so; "may start" invited a band on every tense turn. Now, verbatim: the assistant: "Only when violence is starting or openly threatened, say how hard the fight should be for {name} in the world's terms, with a why: two drunks are trivial, a band of robbers normal, the Watch's full muster impossible. Write it in notes as 'fight: normal, a band of robbers' (trivial, easy, normal, hard, boss or impossible). You name the band; the engine picks the numbers." THIS TURN's offer: "If a fight comes here, the attackers are three robbers. In the story they are just robbers; only inside <<ATTACK>> and <<ENEMY>> calls are they robber_1 to robber_3." (impossible adds: "These are too many to beat. If they come, let the prose show it (their numbers, their ease with steel) and leave {name} room to run or yield; never say so outright."). The narrator's standing rule (prefix, new games): "Anyone THIS TURN offers may come in or be left out; if they come in, your calls use their ids, and the prose describes them in plain words. A monster is known by its name: an owlbear is an owlbear, a skeleton a skeleton; only a person stays a stranger until named." The referee: "If the prose shows someone not in the state attacking the player, file a new person row whose role is the prose's words for them. If they are ones THIS TURN offered, file each one who actually appeared, by its id (robber_1); file none who did not."
- B303 *done (part sixteen §7; answers B293).* X3 was run on 11-09 (run_20261109-02, -03, both arms, 80 turns, before part fifteen) and never reported; run again on today's code on the box, idle for both: the one call and the chain over the 80 Sonnet-keyed turns (run_20261109-04, -05) and the 24 readiness turns (-06, -07). 80 turns, A / B: 14.4 / 15.9 s a turn; kind 76 / 76, time 62 / 58, Sonnet's checks 6/16 / 8/16 with 28 / 45 extra, carried missed 14 / 14; defaults left 43 / 44 (pivotal 0 / 0, tempo 7 / 1, fiat 5 / 12, settled 0 / 0). The chain's messages: the reading 8.9 s (prompt_n 6,883, cache_n 2,378), the checks 5.0 s (884, 9,223), the stakes 4.4 s (1,097, 10,210); every later message's cache grew, none prefilled twice (118 of 118; 46 of 46 on the 24). 24 turns, A / B / Sonnet: 13.5 / 16.2 / 4.7 s; checks found 12/19 / 10/19 / 6/19; defaults left 25 / 16 / 51. The page: https://claude.ai/artifact/1Cqh4BQhqRneDRpV2wQxqS. Not adopted (no 60/40 decisions); the dial stays off until the owner decides.
- B304 *built (the owner, 11-10: "make sure the npc ally is strong there, it's a game design question"; the answer to keeping the story's count).* The Quiet Dead gains Sister Maren, Warden of the Temple of the Quiet Flame (a veteran: AC 17, 58 hit points, a flanged mace; humanoid, not a fanatic; attitude 1): she comes with Wick in the reach scene and says she goes down with whoever goes, and two events (the Old Channel, the Catacombs) bring her to the player's side and into the company while the dead are not put back; the reward raises her attitude. Measured, level 1, four pinned skeletons, the generator choosing the variant: alone the rogue 28% at feeble, the barbarian 43%, the paladin 68%; with Maren the rogue 73% (weak), the barbarian 77% (weak), the paladin 95% (veteran skeletons). lint_pinned_fights counts an ally a quest's event brings to the player's side; the catacomb fight no longer flags (it does with her events removed). Saves already holding the quest keep their frozen cast without her.
- B305 *built (the owner, 11-10: "make the keep seeds when undoing stuff a checkbox in menu, off by default ... when player hits undo that seed should get scrambled").* Settings has an Undo section with "Keep the dice on undo", off by default and server-wide. Off, each undo writes a fresh salt on its undo row and the undone turns, played again, roll on it (Engine.dice_key); on, the turn rolls as it last did (H4 as built). A redo gives its undo's salt back; a rewrite and an eval replay keep the turn's key. D205.
- B306 *built (Gemma fight round 1, 11-10, 4d6f4c683fd3, p16g-catacombs: a level-1 Paladin dead at T12 in the first fight; the owner: "another game or two yourself on Gemma testing fighting mechanics reliability").* T11: "attack" at a foe with a longsword came with attack_at empty and a STR check, so the blow rolled as a skill check; a natural 1 cost five hit points (B274's failure harm) and no line showed the number. T12: "I lay my hand on my own wound" missed Lay on Hands' words ("lay hands"), the lever the assistant named was dropped, the referee spent a point of the pool and its +5 was skipped (hit points are the fight's), so the paladin stayed at 1 and the next 1-damage grip killed him. T10: Turn Undead rolled twice (the reading's 'Turn Undead, CHA medium' beside the lever's own die), the player saw the reading's roll and a bare '§ Turn Undead'. Built: settle_attack takes an intent with a weapon (or the action 'attack') at named targets and a STR or DEX check as a blow (attack_at the targets, the check dropped); the typed-lever gate is engine.pipeline.asks_for (each word of a phrase opens a word of the input, a plural as its singular, filler words skipped); a check the reading labelled with a typed lever is dropped (the lever is the roll); a heal's 'On' defaults to the player; the referee no longer spends a power's count (a count a command owns, and spell slots); a failed check's hit points are on its § line ('It costs 5 hit points.'). Open from the round: the narrator told the wounded skeleton 'collapses back into a heap' while the engine had it hurt (a prose kill the engine never made); a 1-damage blow told as a crushed windpipe and a severed artery; a fumble underground raised Heat by 5 for 'the Watch may hear'; a feature's own dice (Turn Undead, Cure Wounds) are seeded by the player's name and the clock, not the turn's key, so an undo does not reroll them (D205).
- B307 *open: the owner's call, with B290 at the table chat (same round, T8).* The referee filed the crypt's skeleton as `new person the_drowned` (the id of the Mire's drowned band, attitude -3, at The Mire) and moved it to the Narrow Crypt; the engine merged the new row into the existing band ('new person ... is the_drowned: nothing added') and moved the band across the city. The fight then ran on the drowned's block: three bodies, 'rotten grip', and a grab whose peril is 'a pull under the chapel water' (drowning) in a dry crypt; the prose said skeletons in shrouds, the dice said 'the drowned of the Mire chapels'. A narrow fix (an exact-id 'new person' who is elsewhere and shares no word with the note is not that person) needs the same verdict's move row remapped; left for the table chat's ruling on invented enemies.
- B308 *built in part (Gemma fight round 2, 11-10, 8eb1928e28b7, p16g-streets: a level-1 Barbarian, 20 turns, five fights, alive).* About half the player's blows rolled nothing. T10-T11: two punches at the wiry sailor (attack_at set) rolled nothing because the referee's 'present' had put the bar's sailors in player.with_them and the fight join skips anyone there; T5, T10: a punch rolled the carried greataxe (weapon '' or 'fist' fell back to the best carried); T17: 'intimidate the man in velvet', a CHA check with attack_at the offer's shadow, brought a Shadow in and rolled a greataxe blow nobody typed; T15: a stranger brought in by the id 'the_wealthy_stranger' was named 'The_wealthy_stranger'. Built: a blow the reading aims at someone in the company takes them out of it and into the fight; a weapon word or the player's words for an unarmed blow (punch, kick, fist ...: combat.UNARMED) roll the fist; an intent with a CHA, WIS or INT check is not a blow (settle_attack clears its attack_at); a brought-in name has no underscores. Open: T12 'the sailor with the belaying pin' matched no one and the narrator called the offer's robber_1 (the offer's ids stood in for the bar's sailors, 'Robber 2' said aloud in the prose, his block's scimitar for a broken bottle; B290's ground); T15 the reading set attack_at on 'hide and wait for a target' (a Gemma habit: the attack field filled beside a non-blow); T15 Kragga's cleave carried through into the man she was mugging (in the fight by that attack_at) and killed him, and on T17 he handed over his coat; T13-T14 the enemy's rolled blows (4 and 1 damage) were left out of the prose; T14 a flee by STR check left the fight open (the Flee lever was not named); T20 a punch with no target ids was a STR check. Not faults: Rage lasted 55 minutes inside one engine place (the scene rule is an hour or a move); the Shadow's AC moves were the player's AC (Rage, Unarmored Defense).
- B309 *built in part (Gemma fight round 3, 11-10, adcf1869ec54, p16g-verify: a level-1 Paladin, 20 turns, on 51c5940).* The B306 and B308 fixes held: every punch rolled the fist against AC, 'lay my hand on my wound' was Lay on Hands (+5), a word-only threat stayed words. Found: the bestiary matcher read a place in a role ('Keeper of the Drowned Lantern': Maud the innkeeper was a zombie, AC 8, undead) and a label as a monster (the Lamplighter hook's actor, `called: Shadow`, was a Shadow: round 2's Shadow 'out of nowhere' at T17 was him); a bar-room punch killed Old Hesk (4 damage on a commoner's 4); Turn Undead was spent on an empty crypt; Lay on Hands' and Turn Undead's § lines were the bare name. Built: a person with a role is matched on the role's head only (before 'of', 'at', 'in' ...), the label, name and description only for someone with no role; a blow with the fist spares (down, not dead); Turn Undead with nothing dead here is not spent; the heal's and the turning's lines carry the amount and the roll. No real save held the wrong blocks (Lilith's store checked read-only; only the test round 8eb1928e28b7). Open: T19 a sword swing at a corpse that only rose in the same turn's prose had no target id and was a STR check (no fight); an enemy's attack is named by its block's weapon ('Hazel Oakhew's arrows' point-blank, 'Old man's club' for a bony hand); the roll lines name the drowned 'the drowned of the Mire chapels (the first)' (their content name; a 'called' label exists); the sexton's willow grave was the Mire's drowned again (B307's merge).
- B310 *built in part (Gemma fight round 4, 11-10, 187ae951f07d, p16g-verify2: a level-1 Paladin, dead at T17, on 0793350).* Held: every punch rolled the fist, every sword blow an attack roll, the sexton's blows rolled, Maud and Anselm stayed people. Found: Turn Undead's preview rolled 18 (the narrator told the dead turned) and its commit after the prose rolled 6 (failure, applied): a feature's dice were seeded by the clock, which the turn's minutes move (Flee had the same fault, B289); T14 the reading put the weapon in attack_at ('the_drowned', 'longsword') and the engine brought in a commoner called Longsword who swung a club for two turns. Built: a feature's dice are seeded by the last finished turn and its count before spending (features.run), so preview and commit roll alike; settle_attack drops an attack_at entry that is the intent's own weapon. Then (the owner, 11-10: "an item is never a target unless it has some kind of tag that makes it attackable"): combat.is_item, a weapon word, an unarmed word, something carried or a thing in the world is never fought (world_turn filters the struck before any fight join or bring_in); the attackable tag is on ROADMAP area 8, low priority. Open: T16 a flee written as a STR check failed and its 1 hit point (B274's failure harm, now on the § line) took the paladin to 0; the prose had him knocked down, T17 told him crawling out, then the ending said he died (B300's ground: the death told a turn late); T11-T12 the temple vault's dead were filed as the Mire's drowned again and their grab's drowning peril put a pool of black water in a dry vault (B307); T15 a destroyed body's grab was called and told; the roll lines' '(the first)' labels echoed in the prose ('the first of them'); the sexton refused to come, then fought beside him, and struck on a turn the player told him to stay back; 'Out of reach: out of reach of ...' reads twice; the man by the pillar's blows were rolled as a club for a fist (B309's open item).
- B311 *built (the owner, 11-10: "Yes do all the things", on the fixes proposed from the four Gemma fight rounds).* (1) A failed check's cost that takes the last hit point is the death now (the check's note sees the hit points this turn's calls took; the cost joins the turn's tally), not a turn later (B300's told-late death); a blow the prose left out, rolled after it (the backstop), is shown after the prose as the engine's § line (B300's untold blow; 187ae951f07d T15's 7 to 1), and the row no longer repeats it. (2) The referee's check_ignored names a blow's § line, verbatim: "a blow's § line is a called check too: the foe that line names, left hurt or missed by it, told dead, destroyed or out of the fight for good, or a hit told as doing no harm at all, is this rule (knocked down, staggering or giving ground is not);". (3) An enemy's library weapon is named only when their own words carry it, else 'blow' (combat.shown_harm). (4) B307 built: a 'new person' under the id of someone elsewhere whose note shares no word with them is a new person named by the note's head ('Skeleton', 'Mud-slicked corpse'); the report's other rows and its present list follow (Writer.remap). (5) The offer matched by kind keeps the scene's name ('Man with the belaying pin', not 'Robber 2'), one body for one. (6) aim_blows (a game hook before settle_attack): a swing with no one named while fighting is at the foe; a plain run in a fight (run, flee, escape, get away, get out, retreat, bolt, leg it, make a break) read as a STR or DEX check is the Flee lever. (7) The attack fields reworded after a fresh-eyes read, verbatim: attack_at "A blow the player strikes at someone, with a weapon or a fist, in a fight or to start one: whom it is at, by the person's id only, never the weapon or a thing (the weapon goes in weapon); two or three ids only when the words name them all. A blow is never a check: no check and no difficulty. Set it whenever the words strike a blow, even when they also run, grab or shout (\"I run at him and swing\" is a blow). Empty when the words strike no blow: talking, threatening, waiting, hiding."; weapon "With attack_at: the weapon as they carry it, in a word or two; 'fist' only when the words say punch, kick, fist or bare hands. Then after a comma anything the player named that rides on the blow (\"longsword, smite\"). Empty when they name no weapon and nothing rides."; a real Sonnet extract took the schema (run_20261110-01). (8) 'Away: out of reach of ...' for the doubled line; a class power's own dice take the turn's undo salt (pipeline.dice_salt, D205). (9) tools/replay_fights.py (show, check) with the regression set notes/eval/fight_cases.yml: 8 of 8 pass. Not done: the bands' '(the first)' labels (the prose's 'the first of them' reads well; the temple's dead no longer carry the Mire's name); the ally who struck after 'stay back'; Heat from a fumble underground.
- B312 *built (Gemma fight round 5, 11-10, e933e85fb6c2, p16g-verify3: a level-1 Barbarian, 20 turns, alive, on dd12027).* Held: every blow at someone the engine tracked rolled (fist and greataxe), the undead fight's every enemy blow rolled and the hit points matched, the flee ended the fight. Found: half the player's blows rolled nothing, every one at someone not on the panel. A knockout was stored as a death: the § line said 'Sailor 1 is down' and the turn's end made him dead (the hit record lost its spare: older than today, every knockout since part fifteen); so the next punches, aimed at 'sailor_2' (matched by likeness to the fallen Sailor 1) and at sailor_1 himself, rolled nothing; blows at the knife-man went to robber_1, dead the turn before; the flee's line said each enemy got a blow and showed no roll; the catacomb's 'skeletal things' merged with the Mire's drowned on the one shared word 'things'. Built: the hit carries spare to the turn's end (down, not dead); a blow aimed at someone dead, or at a fallen one only by likeness, brings in another like them (their block, their name); one target struck once; the flee's line carries its rolls; B307's shared-word test skips thing, figure, shape, creature, person, people; 'skeletal' is a skeleton's word; a role's head stops at 'with' too. tools/replay_fights.py check lands the stored referee rows on request (verdict: true); the regression set holds 12 cases, 12 pass. Open: 'Sailor 1' numbered when alone; the bands' '(the first)' labels; the save line's 'the third's a pull under the chapel water' (a harm phrase read as a name); the ally who struck after 'stay back'; Heat from a fumble underground.
- B271 *flagged, eased by B278: the budget is tuned on the Paladin.* At level 1 alone the Rogue wins "fair" (two watchmen) 43% and "hard" 11%: the formula does not know the class. A per-class factor, or a band down for the light classes, is the owner's call.

**Added 2026-11-10, the combat review (an independent read of the combat code, the combat record and the six fight rounds 0f734f5c0733, 4d6f4c683fd3, 8eb1928e28b7, adcf1869ec54, 187ae951f07d, e933e85fb6c2; the report docs/REVIEW-2026-11-10-combat.md; numbered from B313). Each was shown by a probe, a ledger query or a replay; no model was called.**

- B313 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Serious.* The narrator's call cap is smaller than a round: `Game.max_calls` is 3 (engine/game.py:445, the fantasy does not raise it), the round list says every listed action "is rolled by the engine when you reach it", the fourth call is answered "No more calls this turn ... Resolve this in the prose, without a roll" (engine/narration.py:179), and the backstop then rolls the same action after the prose. Nine fight turns in the six rounds hit the cap (0f73 T12, T14; 4d6f T9, T11; 8eb T17; 187ae T11, T14, T15; e933 T18); 187ae951f07d T14: the prose has the second corpse "slamming into you", the backstop rolls it a miss. Fix: in a fight the cap is the round's listed actions plus the assessed checks plus one (a game hook reading `state.combat.plan`); until then the cap's reply to a listed action says it is rolled after the prose and its outcome is not to be told. Built: the fantasy's `max_calls_now` hook sizes the cap to the round in a fight (the round's acts and two more, at most twelve; 3 out of one).
- B314 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Serious.* Cleave-through never follows a rider: `resolve_attack` reuses `how` as the loop variable for the spent riders (games/fantasy/combat.py:857 `for fid, how in rider_spent`), so the cleave test at combat.py:865 (`how in ("melee", "finesse")`) is false after any Smite lands, and for every later roll that turn (Extra Attack). Probe: a Smite hit for 35 on a 13 hit point skeleton carried nothing to the second; the same kill by a plain critical carried 6. Fix: rename the loop variable; a test that a Smite kill with damage left over cleaves. Built: the rider loop's variable renamed (`paid`); a Smite kill with damage left over cleaves (test); the calibration table re-recorded (a lone paladin vs 3 skeletons 66.7 to 83.3, vs 4 23.3 to 43.3).
- B315 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Serious.* The generator ranks on noise: every content band (`combat.scale`) and every cold miss (`encounter_for`) is ten simulated fights a cell, and D203's "the nearest at or above the target" then picks lucky cells, so erring easier errs harder. The pinned three zombies at normal (70%) for a level-1 Paladin were sized veteran in 4d6f T9 (70%, alone), adcf T20 (70%, alone) and 187ae T11 (80%, with the sexton); at 600 fights a cell a Paladin alone wins feeble 99.0, weak 97.7, standard 84.3, veteran 52.3. Twenty ten-fight cells of a fight truly won 10% read 0 to 30. Fix: size content bands off the turn (the warm job) at a few hundred fights a cell; choose by a lower confidence bound at or above the target (or demand a margin); a cold miss on the turn takes the easier variant when two overlap. Built: a story's pinned band 60 fights a cell (about a second, first fight only), a cold miss 20; a cell counts as at or above the target only by its lower bound (one standard error); the three zombies now come out standard at about 90%, not a lucky veteran.
- B316 *built in part (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Serious.* The simulator measures a different fight from the one played: (a) `encounter.spawn` builds pinned bodies from the bestiary entry alone (encounter.py:209 `CombatBlock(block=e)`), dropping the content's own block, so the_drowned's grab with a drowning peril (notes/content/fantasy/scenarios/marrowgate.yml:489) is never simulated; (b) `sim_fight` lands only hit points (`combat.land`): a grab's peril, a song's compulsion, a drain's condition never happen in it; (c) the simulated player never pays a failed check's cost. The_drowned killed all three players who died in the six rounds (0f73 T15, 4d6f T12, 187ae T17), each with something the sim does not model (a drowning opening, a check's cost of 7, a failed flee check's last point). Fix: simulate the content block as stored; perils and conditions in the sim (the countdown, the escape as the player's turn); name the sim's blind spots in the encounter row's reason. Built (a, b): a pinned content band is simulated with the content's own moves (Want.actions; the drowned's grab), and the simulator plays a grab's peril (the turn goes to getting free, a lethal one counts down): three zombies standard 87% with the library's moves, 62% with the drowned's grab. Not built (c): the simulated player still never pays a failed check's cost.
- B317 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner, 11-10 night: "Shouting should be a free action ... When in doubt never assume it!"). Serious; amends B100, the owner's call.* In a peril, any action whose words carry no ESCAPE or ATTACK word is death (games/fantasy/world.py:201); the reading (`chose_death`) is consulted only for the LETHAL words. Probe: held, "HAZEL! Help me!" and "I pray to the Quiet Flame" each end "Brin dies: held fast by something stronger, and no fight left in them". A shout for help in a zombie's grip is no chosen death at a kids' table. Fix: in a peril an attempt that is no escape costs a moment of the countdown; death only when the words choose it (LETHAL) and the reading agrees; test_in_a_peril_an_attempt_that_is_no_escape_ends_it changes with it. Built: in a peril, words with no escape or attack are free (the moment does not run) and never a chosen death; then (the owner: "a countdown and then damage over time ... CON saves to avoid it coming to damage", D214) a peril's moments are grace, each failed save spending one, and with none left each failed save hurts by the peril's step (world.DOT, peril_tick: drowning and fire a solid blow, cold and bleeding a scrape, a fall once and over, a hold none); it never ends by itself, death only through the hit points. Then (the owner: "Once you have failed and are burning/drowning you don't continue to get saving throws ... The saving throw is to stay out of bad status", D215) no save once in: the grace (a held breath 1, the first chill 1, fire, bleeding and a hold 0) runs a moment a turn, then each moment hurts; getting out is an action roll (the attempt's own, or the peril's stat when the words go for the way out with none); the status line says '1 moment before it hurts' or 'hurting each moment until out'. Then (the owner: "3 turns of holding breath before it becomes CON check every turn to avoid being in drowning state"; "any peril start immediately after the fail", D216) holding breath is its own status (three safe moments, then a CON check each moment, a failure turning it to drowning); drowning, burning, freezing and bleeding start at the failed save and hurt every moment, no save; a failed way out from a harming peril rolls the moment's harm with it, so a death is told that turn (world.peril_moment). Then (the owner: "CON + 1 min 2"; "once you hit drowning/freezing/etc you do CON checks every turn to avoid damage, but as soon as you fail you take damage every round. No more checks"; burning "dex checks to put it out ... We don't want every spell with burning damage to cause this", D217) holding breath is CON + 1 moments, at least 2; drowning and freezing check CON each moment until the first failure (Peril.failing), then harm every moment; burning and bleeding harm every moment until put out (a DEX attempt) or bound; burning opens only from content (no fire damage opens it); world.moment decides a moment once for the world turn, the escape roll's note and the simulator. Then (the owner: "Is it clear how and possible for the player to put themselves out if on fire, swim to the surface ..., staunch a wound"; a probe found 8 of 12 natural ways out did nothing, the word list sending them to 'free'; D218) any check in a peril is the way out tried, a deed with no roll rolls the peril's way-out stat (DEX fire, WIS a wound, STR water, high while held under, CON the cold), only speech is free; water, a bandage or healing, and warmth end their perils with no roll; healing binds a wound; a WARNING names the way out when a peril opens and the status chip names it; the referee opens and ends perils ('+on fire', a peril of none). The 13 probe phrasings now all behave (tests/test_deadly.py).
- B318 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Serious; B290's ground, B309's rule not carried over.* The bestiary matcher reads a whole description: `bring_in` -> `named_entries(words)` -> `match_entry` over the full phrase (encounter.py:113, combat.py:101). Probe: 'the man in the shadows' is a Shadow (undead), 'a bear of a man' a black bear, 'the woman with the dog' a wolf, 'the boy with the boar spear' a boar, 'the dead man's brother' undead by kind. B309 cut a role to its head (`_ROLE_TAIL`); the words path has no cut. Fix: the same head cut on the words, and an entry only when it is the head noun; 'X of a man' and a person's noun (man, woman, boy, fence) stay humanoid. Built: `encounter.noun_phrase`: the words' head noun (before of, in, with ...; after a possessive; 'X of a man' a man) is what the bestiary reads.
- B319 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Serious.* `count_in` reads any word of four letters or more ending in s as a plural (encounter.py:471 `\w{3,}s`), and 'dead' anywhere: a plural with no number is the generator's to size, two at least. Probe: `bring_in(s, 'hollis', named=True)` wrote in six commoners, Townsman 1 to 6, all joined to the fight; 'Marcus the smith', 'the priestess', 'the abbess', 'the boss' the same. Fix: a plural only by the head noun's form (not after s, u or i: 'boss', 'Marcus', 'abbess'), a proper name is one person, and an unnamed plural's default is two, not the generator's six. Built: a plural ends in s after anything but s, i or u ('hollis', 'the abbess', 'Marcus the smith' are one); a head ending in 'dead' is many; the unnamed plural's size stays the generator's (the record §12b).
- B320 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Serious; a side effect of B311.* A blow at a corpse brings in a new enemy: world.py:368-379 spawns "another like them" whenever attack_at names someone dead. Probe: "I stab the dead robber again to make sure he stays down" (attack_at robber_1, dead) writes in Robber 2 and opens a fight. Fix: only when the target was matched by likeness (not the dead one's exact id), or when the turn's words or the prose show another of them; a blow at a body is no roll and no fight. Built: an exact dead id with the player's words at the body (again, make sure, body, corpse, remains, finish off, stays down) brings no one in.
- B321 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Minor.* Flee with a destination takes no free blows: world_turn's contact break (world.py:192) clears the fight before the Flee lever commits, `run_fight_command` then answers "There is no fight to leave", and the lever's preview (rolled before world_turn) had told the narrator blows that never land. Probe: "I run for it, out to <place>" with Flee: no blows; "I run for it": two blows rolled. Flee also strikes with only the first two bodies of a band (combat.py:1093). Fix: when Flee is picked, world_turn leaves the fight to the lever; every body in reach strikes, or the rule says why not. Built: world_turn leaves contact to a picked Flee; the band's two-body limit kept and said in the lever's hint ('at most two of a band').
- B322 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Minor; B273's other half.* THIS TURN contradicts itself on a fight's first round: `turn_directives` runs on the state before world_turn, so the could-turn line ("Here, not fighting X now ... Otherwise they do not act against X") names the foes the round list has in the fight: 5 of 36 fight bulletins, the opening round of every the_drowned fight (0f73 T12, 4d6f T9, 187ae T11, adcf T20, e933 T18); at 8eb T13 the part-four "In a fight with ... write <<ENEMY>>" line rode beside the round list. Fix: build the standing fight lines after world_turn, or drop from them every id the plan holds. Built: WorldTurn.replaces: a turn whose round is planned drops the standing 'Here, not fighting' and 'In a fight with' lines and writes the could-turn line again without the round's foes (`world.could_turn_line`, one home).
- B323 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Minor (debt).* Two enemy resolvers: an <<ENEMY>> call before a fight is planned (`s.combat` None) rolls the part-four path (world.world_call: the person's `moves` or DEFAULT_STRIKE +2, damage by step, holds and songs as checks with difficulty words), not the person's SRD block. 8eb1928e28b7 T12: "Robber 2's blow: 8 +2 = 10 against armour 11" for a bandit whose block is +3, 1d6+1; Gorm's opening blow would be a scrape. Fix: every ENEMY call through `combat.resolve` (open `s.combat` and a join entry, as world.py:493 does in a fight); delete the part-four strike path, `_pick_move`, DEFAULT_STRIKE and `_blow`. Built: every <<ENEMY>> goes through `combat.resolve` and the person's block (outside a fight on a passing round; the fight joins after the prose by the result's `join`); `_pick_move` deleted; the part-four `_blow` stays for the hazards.
- B324 *open (combat review, 11-10). Minor (debt).* Damage reduction lives in four places with different numbers: `world.take` (a shield caps a blow at 5, `step_down`), `game._hp` (the referee's rows: a cap of 10 or half, a shield cap of 5, Rage, Uncanny Dodge), `combat._player_take` (STEP_DICE cuts 3/2/1, the shield only on 6 or more, Defence, Shield of Faith), `world._blow`; and the shield also adds +2 AC (`world.armour_class`), so it counts twice against an attack roll. Two Sneak Attacks (`game._sneak_attack` for checks, `combat._sneak_case` for rolls) and two finesse lists (game.FINESSE/HEAVY, combat.WEAPONS). Fix: one function per rule in combat.py; the shield as AC only (SRD) or the step only, the owner's pick. Shield built (the owner, 11-10 night: "remove the shield glitch as it's probably part of why the paladin is so op compared to other classes"): the shield is its +2 AC only against an attack roll (combat._player_take); it still cuts hazard and failure damage, which no roll faced. At 600 fights vs three skeletons, level 1 alone: paladin 48, barbarian 14, rogue 8 (the paladin was 79 before); the calibration table re-recorded; the pinned-fight lint still clean. The four reduction homes are not merged yet.
- B325 *built in part (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Minor (debt).* Dead and vestigial combat code: `if True:` at combat.py:512 and encounter.py:455; `roles.AllyOrder` and `AttackSpec.ally_orders` (never set; orders come from `ally_orders_in`); `combat.budget`, BANDS, DIFFICULTY and MAX_BODIES ("kept for the pages"); world.backstop's part-four loop (only when `s.combat` is None, which plan_fight prevents in a fight); the record's enemy surprise (`CombatState.surprise == "enemies"`) exists only as a comment. Fix: delete, or build the surprise as the record §3 says. Built in part: the old budget (BANDS, DIFFICULTY, MAX_BODIES, budget), the `if True:` branches, `roles.AllyOrder` and `AttackSpec.ally_orders`, `_pick_move` deleted. Kept: world.backstop's part-four loop (still reached by a hostile present outside a fight); the enemy surprise not built.
- B326 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Minor.* The referee writes the player's hit points in fights: 17 `add player.hp` rows in the six rounds (4d6f T12 "-10 throat crushed"), none applied, but only because some call that turn carried harm (engine/pipeline.py:1300) or a lever had applied it; a fight turn whose rolls all missed while the prose told a hit would land one. Fix: skip player.hp on every turn with a round planned; the referee's player-hit-point rule says a fight's are the engine's. Built: the game's `fight_owns_hp` hook: any turn with a fight on skips the referee's player hit points.
- B329 *designed (the owner, 11-10: diffs, with the last ten turns kept whole and a full keyframe every twentieth turn; was proposed: "That size is not acceptable. How can we fix it? ... needs to be in roadmap"; every game).* The save grows without end and a full snapshot of it is stored every turn: saves 82 to 121 KB by turn 50 in every game, 914 KB at week 40 in the clown's simulator; round 4's snapshots 4.3 MB for 50 turns; snapshots 237 MB of the 643 MB database. A turn changes about 1 KB. The fix, in order (ROADMAP §7): compress the snapshot (105 KB to 24 KB); snapshots as differences with a full one every tenth turn; the save bounded (fired one-shot events folded to ids, the log a window, histories capped); retention for the ledger's exchanges and old test games. The engine's speed is not the issue (2 ms a conversion, 10 ms a load at week 40). Found by the clown chat's simulator work (CLOWN_ROADMAP C97). Measured: a diff 0.1 ms, a rebuild 1 to 2 ms; a full save every 10 turns with compressed diffs between, 346 KB for round 4's 50 turns against 4.4 MB (ROADMAP §7). The database side (the owner: "That bloat?"): 643 MB, of it the snapshots 240, the ledger 211 (the `model_call` rows 125, 87% a second copy of the prompt already in the blob table), the blobs 138; the fix adds a request named by its blob hash, compressed blobs and prompt diffs, retention for test games, and VACUUM (ROADMAP §7, steps 5 to 8). The owner's bar: a few gigabytes is fine, growth faster than the turns is not; the must-do items are the diffs and the request by its hash, the rest optional.
- B327 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Minor (debt).* ATTACK (games/fantasy/game.py:71) has no closing word boundary: 'cutpurse' is 'cut', 'hitch' 'hit', 'loosen' 'loose', "Fighter's" 'fight'. Probe: `breaks_contact` refuses "I slip away from the cutpurse into the crowd" (without 'cutpurse' it breaks); the same regex gates the peril death (B317), the compulsion floor and the fight join. Fix: `\b` at the end with the inflections listed (strikes, stabbed, swinging). Built: ATTACK closed at the word's end with its inflections ('cutpurse', 'hitch', 'loosen', "Fighter's" no longer match).
- B328 *built (combat review, 11-10; the owner: "let's get them fixed"). Minor (tooling).* The call report has no fight table: the record §10 asked for "per fight turn, the calls made against the actions listed, and the backstop's share"; tools/round_report.py has none, and the mechanics row's `calls` mixes the narrator's calls with the backstop's with no flag (the review counted from the ledger's world rows). Fix: a per-round table in round_report: listed, called, capped, backstopped, aimed blows unrolled, the player's hit points by source (narrated blow, backstop, a check's cost). Built: `round_report.fight_table`: per fight turn the acts called, rolled after the prose, the cap hit, and the player's hit points by source; round 4 (187ae951f07d): 44% called, 2 hit points to told blows, 8 after the prose, 1 to a check.
- B330 *built (the owner, 11-10 night: "'Grab' is a terrible example for a peril then. Most grabs are just ways to immobilize player and do damage right?").* An enemy's grab grapples and does its damage (combat.resolve; the block's or the action's dice): the player is held (the grappled condition, `Condition.by` the grabber) and cannot Flee until a Strength or Dexterity attempt to break free succeeds or the grabber is down, dead or out of the fight; no second grab on the grappled; a grab never opens a peril (a `hold` with a peril still does: the sirens). Content: the drowned's grab is 'a cold grip that drags you down', 1d4; the mire-croc's 'jaws that clamp and hold', 1d6. Three drowned at weak, level 1 alone: paladin 91, barbarian 58, rogue 27.5.
- B331 *built (the owner, 11-10 night: "we need to be making 'weak' much more weak ... Let's not show 'weak' during fight ... we can show 'huge' ... we need more variation levels").* The ladder (encounter.LADDER, VARY): feeble ×0.35 −3, frail ×0.5 −2, weak ×0.7 −1 (DC too), standard, veteran ×1.25 +1, elite ×1.5 +2; every candidate measured at weak, standard, veteran; any fight still too hard steps down to frail, then feeble; a boss may go up to elite. Names: below standard nothing, veteran 'Big' (a monster) or 'Tough' (a person), elite 'Huge' or 'Fearsome'. Four skeletons, level 1 alone (feeble/frail/weak/standard): paladin 71/62/38/16, barbarian 47/30/11/1, rogue 50/40/14/0, fighter 42/29/4/0; the catacomb quest's skeletons are winnable only with Sister Maren for the non-paladins (the lint now tests a six-skeleton case for 'deadly'). Then (the owner: "we should have a minion level ... maybe like 1d4 halved, rounding up", D216) minion at the bottom: a band's bodies at ceil(1d4/2) hit points each, their blows 1d4 at attack and DCs -2, for two bodies or more; four skeletons as minions, level 1 alone: paladin 99, barbarian 95, rogue 85, fighter 89 (six: 89, 72, 45, 57); the pinned-fight lint measures a band at minion.
- B332 *built (the owner, 11-10 night: "Warning messages every turn"; "We should have a debug room in the game for these Gemma/sonnet runs - somewhere the player can go to fight anything or get hit by anything ... your call").* A peril's WARNING is a § line every turn the player is in it (the game's `player_alerts`). The test hook (dev servers only, TA_DEV_MODE; Lilith's has none): a turn opening `DEBUG:` is the engine's before any model (`typed_command`, `_debug`): room (the Glass Cellar), spawn <creature> [count] [rung], set me on fire, drown me, make me bleed, freeze me, push me off, grab me, hurt N, heal, clear; the narrator plays it in a line; no button. The player's brief and TESTING list it.
- B333 *built in part (Gemma round 6, 11-10 night, 6c793b6930d1, p16g-perils: a Fighter on the lake and in the Mire, 20 turns, alive, on ddb803f).* Held: the holding-breath chip named the way out; STR swam up; a plea to Tam rolled his STR and pulled the player out (T18). Found: the lake's cold water was still the old hazard (a CON check every turn in the water, each failure a scrape and 'chilled', -4 then -6 for mail), against the owner's rule; no WARNING line at any point (the every-turn line came after this round, B332); the narrator surfaced the player against his words (T4: 'let her pull me down' became 'kick away and surge upward', which broke contact) and told drowning the engine did not have (T8); the causeway's huge failure put the player under a turn before the prose said so (T13); T17 the prose had both drowned sink away while the backstop after it rolled an 8-point critical grip (9 to 1 hit points). Built: a hazard rolls nothing while the player is in a peril; cold water's failure opens freezing (content: fail {peril: freezing}, huge_fail drowning). Open: the narrator ending a fight or a peril in prose the engine still has (B300's ground; the referee may now file '-drowning' or a peril of none for a rescue, and check_ignored covers a blow's line).
- B334 *built in part (Gemma round 7, 11-10 night, f6428fd5c68b, p16g-debug: a Barbarian through the DEBUG hook, dead at T14, on db2cb2f).* Held: spawning, heal, clear, minions dropping in one hit (and cleaving through), the names plain ('Skeleton 1'), the grab stopping Flee. Found: `DEBUG: room` broke the turn after the reading (the Glass Cellar put in found_places as a bare string: _same_spot crashed); every DEBUG peril escaped on its own turn (the debug turn's text read as an attempt, a way-out roll made at once), so no turn in danger was tested; spawned foes struck on the spawn turn (14 to 6 hit points before the player acted); a grab showed no WARNING and no chip; 'I wrench my arms out' was read as a punch and no roll ended the grab, so the zombie's next blow killed at 0. Built: the room as a FoundPlace; a DEBUG turn's world turn does nothing (it sets up; nothing acts until the next turn); a grapple's WARNING each turn and a 'Held by X: break free (STR or DEX)' chip; words that go for breaking free roll STR or DEX (the better) and a success ends the grab. Open: the dead foes listed as hostile in the People line; 'bloodied' covering 6/14 and 2/14; death at 0 hit points with no dying state (the owner's rule, D178).
- B335 *built (Gemma round 8, 11-10 night, 62e207f3fefa, p16g-debug2: a Fighter through every peril by the DEBUG hook, 24 turns, alive, on 74776ca; the owner: "Can't assistant help match intents?? ... it should try to guess the intent"; "the voice thing is weird ... this room never has anyone besides the player and anyone they spawn"; "jumping in water etc doesn't take a check, that's changing locations").* Found: a CHA shout put the fire out twice and a WIS look ended the drowning (D218's 'any check is the way out' too wide); a stale WARNING after an escape (written before the escape landed); the zombie struck on the DEBUG turn (the narrator's own call); 'I run' while held rolled a break-free; the narrator invented 'the Voice' in the Glass Cellar. Built: a check is the way out when the reading marks it ('way out:' plus the player's words; the assistant's ENGINE NOTE while in a peril or held, read fresh by a subagent) or its stat fits and its words go for it (world.way_out; the word lists the fallback); a deed that does not go for the way out is a moment in the peril; the WARNING written at the turn's end; the DEBUG turn quiet (world_call answers nothing that turn); break-free words only (BREAK_FREE); the Glass Cellar's standing line (nobody but the player and the spawns) and the referee's new person refused there.
- B336 *built (Gemma round 9, 11-10 night, 93a54b141f99, p16g-debug3: the DEBUG hook's dangers again on the B335 build; the owner: "Assistant judging should probably be toggleable like other module type stuff, so it might be turned on for sonnet but not for a local model").* Found: the old ESCAPE word list ended perils it should not ('fire' in a shout and in the reading's summary 'on fire', 'reach' in a search swam the player up); 'sea' matched 'seam' (no closing word boundary); a referee '+bleeding' from any blow opened the bleeding peril; the WARNING went into the status notes, which the turn tool never showed. Built: each peril's own words (world.WAY_WORDS: put out, smother, roll for fire; swim, surface for water; press, bind for a wound; keep moving, rub for cold; grab, cling for a fall), the player's words only (not the summary), closing word boundaries; a referee's bleeding is a wound, not the peril (content and the DEBUG hook open it); the WARNING a § line each turn in danger (player_alerts), quiet on the turn the player's own way out is rolled; hacking free of a grip is a break-free. And the toggle: the assistant role's `game_notes` (engine/config.py, on by default), the profile dial `game_notes` (dials: {game_notes: off}), shown on the profile card; off, the reading gets no game note and the engine's word test alone decides (D221).
- B337 *built (the debt review after round 9, 11-10 night).* The old wide ESCAPE word list (swim, climb, run, stab, fire, warm, reach and forty more) still decided two things after B336 took it out of the way-out test: whether a STR or DEX success freed the player from a grip (a successful 'run for the door' freed them, against B335's 'break-free only by its own words') and whether a failed check in a fight or peril costs hit points. Built: ESCAPE deleted; the grip ends on a 'way out:' mark or BREAK_FREE words (struggle free, get loose added); failure_harm reads an attack, a physical stat, the mark, BREAK_FREE or the peril's own way out; the mark's pattern is one constant (world.MARKED, three copies before); the 'any success' outcome tuple is engine.mechanics.WON (thirteen copies before, four files).
- B338 *built in part (Gemma round 10, 11-10 night, 8085528d0af0, p16g-debug4: a Halfling Rogue through every peril and a grab by the DEBUG hook on the B336/B337 build, 35 turns, alive).* Worked: every way out rolled its stat and ended its peril on a success; the shout with 'fire' in it cost nothing and ended nothing; unrelated deeds left the player in it; breath counted down, then CON checks until one failed, then harm. Found and built: (1) on a grab turn the assistant read 'I run' and 'cut myself loose' as 'break free' with attack_at the zombie, so the engine rolled a blow and the break-free (D222: getting loose is the turn's act; aim_blows clears the blows; the held note says one intent, read fresh by a subagent); (2) T34 'attack' with a dagger, targets set, no check and attack_at empty rolled nothing and the narrator killed the zombie in prose (aim_blows aims it at the foe being fought); (3) T28 the DEBUG fall rolled the narrator's own catch (max_calls_now 0 on the DEBUG turn; the pipeline no longer reads a 0 cap as unset); (4) the WARNING hid on failed way-out turns (shown unless a way out worked this turn: check_note's flags `escaped`/`freed`; the grip's test one helper, world.frees_grip); (5) a look while bleeding, read trivial, cost no moment (a trivial deed in a peril is a moment; only speech is free); (6) a search while on fire took thirty minutes (the minutes_cap hook: a turn in a peril, held or in a fight is at most a minute); (7) the referee took 3 hit points on a free shout while burning (dice_own_hp, renamed from fight_owns_hp, covers perils); (8) a swim took the rapier's finesse bonus ('claw' read as an attack; finesse never on a 'way out:' check); (9) 'clear' left a twisted knee (clears every condition); (10) '1 hit points'; (11) 'drown me' in the Glass Cellar moved the player to the river (the room's line: a live peril happens in the room, read fresh). Open: the narrator's invented gear losses (a burning pack, the armour off, 'your gear gone') never reach the sheet except the armour's place; a dead zombie still listed 'hostile' on the roster; the § line nets the modifiers into one number (DEX read +5, +3, +4, +1 across the run: soaked, a twisted knee, a boot for leverage), so the player cannot see why.
- B339 *built in part (Gemma round 11, 11-10 night, 56dd55904e70, p16g-plain1: ordinary play, no DEBUG; a Human Fighter takes the rat job and the lost girl, 32 turns, alive, Wren home, 57 gold, Renown known).* The first two-thirds read as a good small adventure (each lead from something the player did; the rat fight rolled every blow and matched the story). Found and built: (1) T30 'retreat from the gallery' with attack_at the drowned and no weapon rolled a longsword blow at three drowned the story had not shown (aim_blows drops attack_at from an intent with no weapon and no blow in its words or the player's); (2) T32 carried out to the ferry landing mid-fight, the drowned followed and struck there (leaving for another place in a fight is the Flee lever; a flee pays the XP of foes already downed, which had paid nothing); (3) T28 the referee's '+grappled' for 'grappling with the child' as the player scooped Wren up held the player, 'by something' (a referee grab needs a foe the player fights, named or the only one; else refused); (4) T10 a cold-water hazard's freezing opened after the prose, so that turn had no WARNING (opened when the hazard is rolled); (5) T11 a walk to the inn's fire while freezing took one minute under B338's cap (a move to another place, out of a fight, keeps its travel time); (6) 'Out of it: no longer the cold closing in, the limbs going' read as cut off (world.out_of_it: 'no longer freezing.'). Also built: a dead person's card shows its status, not its attitude (the dead read 'hostile' in rounds 10 and 11; the turn tool prints the status). Open: the quest accepted aloud at T6 never registered because the deck still held 'The Ferryman's Daughter' as dealt while Tam offered it in the prose at T5 (the story ran ahead of the deal; the referee invented 'hook-wren_return' for the acceptance, filed as people.wren and refused): the deck should recognise its own hook offered in the prose by its giver; T31 three drowned acted in the prose when one was alive, and the place jumped to The Drowned Lantern in the tunnel (the narrator and the referee; B300's family); checks nobody needed (STR to row calm water, INT to be told the way, CHA after the troll agreed, CHA to collect a promised reward); narrated gear the sheet never held (a dented shield, a ribbon given away). A design question for the owner: cold water's armour penalty is -2 a step, so chain mail is -8 on the CON check against the cold (T10 read 15 - 6); fine for swimming, harsh for the cold.
- B340 *built in part (Gemma round 12, 11-10 night, 067eadd5207f, p16g-plain2: ordinary play, no DEBUG; an Elf Rogue on the river and the lake, a siren's hoard, a pickpocketing, the channel under the inn; 32 turns, alive, never hurt).* The lake half read well (the song, the stealthy climb, the theft from the siren's pile, the key opening the grate under the inn). Found and built: (1) T19 'follow the stranger quietly' (weapon 'none', attack_at the stranger) rolled a dagger blow that killed the stranger: D223's guard cleared it, then B338's no-check rule read the truthy 'none' as a weapon and aimed it again (the reading's weapon field maps 'none' and its kin to empty, a validator, no schema change); (2) the tester saw none of the loot (a blade, a key, a ring, a pouch) because the turn tool never printed the pockets; all four were on the sheet (the tool prints a Pockets line); (3) the channel under the quay was 'ice-cold' in the prose with no hazard to call (content: cold water in The Old Channel and The Sunken Quarter). Open: a purse lifted and counted stays an item, its coins never gold (T21, T22); gold +1 on a look at the siren's pile (T14) and a 'handful' of coins as 1 gold (T15); Hidden kept after the siren heard (T16 to T18); the place jumped (T2 the Old Bridge for the quay, T10 the river Marrow out on the lake); the journal named people never met (Wren, Mirren, Elowen); the narrator invented a predator, and Tam as an ally that made a Sneak Attack eligible (T19); the same stat's modifier wandered roll to roll (CHA +2 to +4, DEX +7 to +11) with nothing on the line saying why, the third round to flag it (the § line nets the modifiers: a design question for the owner).
- B341 *built in part (Gemma round 13, 11-10 night, 9c6ac4cbe0ae, p16g-plain3: ordinary play, no DEBUG; a Dwarf Paladin to the Mire's drowned chapels and Maud's rats, 32 turns, alive; the drowned fight took him to 1 hit point, broke free, fled to the skiff).* The drowned fight read as the best yet: every blow and grab rolled, the hit points matched the prose. Found and built: (1) the paladin's abilities never showed their line: Turn Undead's CHA roll and Lay on Hands' heal went to the ledger only, the web UI showing just the label (CommandResult.shown, a § line after the prose for a narrated command; the fantasy's features fill it); (2) Divine Smite clicked took a turn with no blow: the narrator smote a swarm the sheet kept alive and the referee spent holy_power from the prose (a rider is an engine command, instant, arming the next blow; the referee never spends a count a class feature owns, its id is not the lever's); (3) T12 the Strength save against the drowned's grab held, and the referee's '+grappled' from the prose held the player anyway (D224: a grab is the dice's; the referee never sets grappled, superseding D223's 'a foe the player fights'); (4) 'the_shadow', never met, offered as a Lay on Hands target (hidden people are no ability targets). Open: Turn Undead said 'nothing dead is here' in the chapel where the drowned were last seen (T28), and turned three drowned the prose raised but the state never filed (T31, T32); the rats quest stayed 'offered' while the player fought the swarms, then paid on the kill that left swarm 2 alive (T22; the deck's acceptance again, B339); both torches vanished when one was dropped (T8); 'a shimmering object' filed on sight, before it was picked up (T29); a stamp of the foot read as its own STR check that cost 2 hit points (T21); 1 of 12 hit points reads 'bloodied' like 6 (a design question: a near-death word); Tam took the player straight to The Mire, skipping the Ferry Landing and the causeway (T4, T5).
- B342 *built (the owner's answers, 11-10 late: "Why is the roll modifier bouncing around? Include the numbers still but check to make sure it's not a glitch"; "like critically wounded or something"; "Nothing for cold, actually, just swimming"; "Yes" to the deck knowing its own job; "give those a shot too", the debt review).* The modifiers were checked on every roll of rounds 12 and 13 and were no glitch: each check takes what bears on it (the assistant's edge, -4 to +4 with its reason; Keen senses, Expertise, Sneak by the label's words; conditions: chilled, soaked), so CHA read +2 bare and +4 with a +2 edge. One real glitch: a grab's DEX -2 counted against the roll to break that same grab (round 13 T13); no longer. Built: the § line names the sum's parts when anything besides the stat counts ('8 + 11 (DEX +5, Sneak +2, Expertise: Stealth +2, the siren is lost in her own song +2) = 19 against 8'; the player's line only); the hit point tiers have 'critically wounded' under a quarter (one home for the tiers, state.hp_word, which had two); armour costs a swimming check -2 a step (a STR or DEX check under water, or a check that swims) and nothing against the cold (content: cold water's armour_penalty gone; the causeway's stays, the footing in the bog); an unknown hook- id the referee writes for a job the prose offered is matched to the dealt or live hook it means by its people and title, and a dealt one comes in as offered and taken up (round 11's 'hook-wren_return'). Debt (docs/REVIEW-2026-11-10-perils-debt.md, "Paid down"): turn_limits, one hook for three; the DEBUG turn one signal; TURN_FLAGS cleared once; the DEBUG hook in games/fantasy/debug.py.
- B343 *built in part (Gemma round 14, 11-10 late, a5cf93d41879, p16g-plain4: ordinary play, no DEBUG; a Half-Orc Fighter in mail takes three jobs, dives for a box; 29 turns, dead at T29 to a rat swarm's critical bite, 15 from 8).* The roll lines' parts added up every time and read as sensible reasons. Found and built: (1) the feeble rat swarm kept its 2d6 bite, so a crit did 15 to a level-1 fighter (the ladder's frail and feeble rungs soften the blows: frail halves the dice, feeble drops a die size too; encounter.soften); (2) T6 the referee's yes to hook-ferrymans_daughter landed but the hook stayed dealt, unseen, all round (a yes or a no to a dealt hook brings it in as offered: its reach fires); (3) T19 "Who's lost something under the water?", said in a tavern, rolled cold water (the hazard backstop skips quoted speech; the Sunken Quarter's words were new tonight); (4) T20 'CON 11 against 12' with CON +2 and chilled -2 netting nought read as a lost +2 (a nought sum names its parts). Open: T21 Gemma labelled the dive into the water itself 'way out:' while freezing, and its CON success ended the cold (the assistant's misjudgment; the game_notes switch is the lever if it recurs); no swim roll on the dive in mail (the assistant set none; the armour penalty only rides a swimming check); 'what Bryn brought +2', an edge with no reason given; the about text's +2 ('too proud to take off her mail') on asking for work; AC 17 for chain mail and a shield (the engine's armour adds DEX: 10 + DEX + 4 + 2, where the table's mail is 16 flat); the rope untied never came back to the pockets, the skiff tied up stayed in them.
- B344 *built in part (Gemma round 15, 11-10 late, b8e96b0c3c1f, p16g-plain5: ordinary play, no DEBUG; a Human Barbarian meant for the Greywood and the Barrow; 14 turns, dead at T14 in the inn cellar to Gorm the troll, never out of the city).* The inn scenes read well; every bracketed sum added up. Found and built: (1) three jobs' reach events stopped three nights' sleep within the hour (T8 to T11; the prose slept to morning each time, the clock moved 4 and 60 minutes): a job's own events (a deck's, `hook` set) no longer wake a sleeping player, the owner's rule that what the player cannot see does not stop the clock; they fire as the sleep ends (clock_stop's `asleep`, pipeline.sleeping: the Rest command's long rest or the sleep words, whatever the tempo); (2) Rage clicked took the turn and gave the troll its second blow (an `instant` feature in the class file, Rage, is an engine lever like a rider: on at once, its line 'Rage: on until the scene ends.'); (3) Rage's step on the troll's blow (14 to 11) reached only the narrator, so the player read 'Rage did nothing' (an enemy blow's player line names the steps, '(Rage: a step lighter)'). Open: a wary Gorm (content: 'a fight to walk away from at level 1', a full troll) lunged at the player off a failed WIS listen check and killed a level-1 barbarian in two blows before he swung (a story person's own block, never sized; whether a wary person may open a fight off a failed check is the owner's call); typed levers commit after the prose, so 'I rage and swing' swings without the rage (a change for every game's typed engine commands, the clown's too); the rats job stayed 'offered' after the player's yes (T12: the referee wrote only the hook's note, and the cellar is no place of its own); the sleep prose repeated nearly word for word on T8, T9 and T11; Hazel's surname borrowed from the sheet.
- B345 *built (the owner, 11-10 late: "One armor at a time and yes dex penalties count. Really need an equipped panel somewhere in ui and ability to equip unequip"; and "Agreed on logged issues": B344's open items stay logged).* What the player wears and holds is now state: each pocket item has `equipped`, one body armour, one shield and one weapon in hand at a time (games/fantasy/gear.py). Armour class reads only what is worn: 10 + DEX (a penalty counts) + the body armour + a shield; the swimming penalty and Defence read only worn armour (round 12's leather pouch had counted as armour and as a step in the water; carried spare armour no longer stacks). Equip and Unequip are instant levers (one of each slot: putting on leather takes the mail off; no buckling on armour in a fight or a danger; anything comes off at any time, shedding mail in deep water); the attack uses the weapon in hand unless the words name another. An Equipped panel (armour, shield, in hand, armour class with its parts), a 'Wearing' line in the models' state block, an Equipped line in the turn tool. An older save wears its best armour, a shield and its best melee weapon until the player changes them.
- B346 *built in part (Gemma round 16, 11-10 late, 6343bc8911d1, p16g-plain6: ordinary play, no DEBUG; a Dwarf Barbarian from the city through the Greywood to the Barrow and back, then Wren home from the sirens; 32 turns, alive, never in a fight).* Smooth and coherent: the place line followed every move, the quest panel tracked every job (offered, turned down, taken up on the spoken yes, done with its pay), the jobs' reaches waited for the waking (D226), every bracketed sum added up. Found and built: the long Rest at 18:32 ran a fixed eight hours to 02:32 while the prose said dawn, and the next morning's walk ran in the night (the owner's rule, a night's sleep runs to morning: the Rest command's long rest runs to 07:00 in the evening or the night, eight hours by day; a night's sleep may run past the turn's 600-minute cap, to sixteen hours; both only for a game that sleeps by command, the fantasy). Open: nearly every turn rolled a check (questions, looks; three failed WIS looks stalled the Barrow search); the Barrow gave up a torque, a casket and a gem with no guardian or cost (no hook dealt for it); 'Hidden' set at the Barrow unasked and kept for three places; an unseen watcher sent word to the city and Heat never moved; the road to Thornhollow took 28 minutes where the prose said an hour and more, the walk back two hours; one 'Ferry Landing' for both banks; a cloak the player threw over Wren that the pockets never held; the narrator decided the player ignored a job pitch (T24); Rage and the fights untested (none came).
- B347 *built in part (Gemma round 17, 11-10 late, 5d85334fc803, p16g-plain7: ordinary play, no DEBUG; a Half-Orc Barbarian with handaxes to the Mire's drowned chapels; 15 turns, dead at T15 on the causeway after fleeing the drowned).* Rage clicked took no turn and printed its line; every blow on him showed '(Rage: a step lighter)'; Equip switched to the javelins and back at once and the throw used the javelin in hand; walking off ended the fight with its parting blows; 'critically wounded' showed at 1 hit point. Found and built: (1) the typed Flee's parting blows (5, to 1 hit point) land after the prose, so the causeway the narrator called mid-flight read 6 hit points and told 'a scrape'; the scrape killed after the prose under a telling that had him clear and safe, and the player never saw the hit point (a command carries `hp_taken`, Flee's own; a typed lever's cost counts in the turn's tally from the start, so a call mid-prose that takes the last hit point is told as the death); (2) no Unarmored Defense: AC 11 with no armour, three drowned hit six of seven (the class file's effect `unarmored_con`: with no body armour worn, CON adds to the armour class, a shield still counts); (3) Rage gave nothing on the blows given (the SRD's +2 damage on a Strength melee blow, named on the line); (4) one javelin thrown cost two (the engine counts an item it spends, thrown or shot, and the referee's count for it that turn is refused). Open: the prose raised four drowned where the sheet had three, and gave the blows to the wrong bodies (T10, T12); a knock-down in the prose with no prone; Tam's promise of his daughter's job never reached the quest panel (the referee wrote no yes); the ribbon went into the pockets unasked (T3); one torch dropped took both; ordinary talk rolled three turns running.
- B348 *built (the owner's night brief, 11-10 late: "Too many rolls is one of the biggest annoyances ... work out these remaining bugs, and if there's no obvious direct fix redesign thing subtly"; first batch, with Gemma pair round G1, 86c23260bece, p17g-pair1: a Fighter on the rats job, 18 turns, dead to a swarm the prose had routed).* (1) Too many rolls: of 112 checks in rounds 12 to 17, 46 were CHA low (a question, failure 'a vague answer') and about 30 WIS looks; in a calm scene (a game's opt-in `settle_calm`: nobody hostile here, no peril, no fight) a low check with nothing at stake works with no roll, a CHA/WIS/INT one even with Gemma's habitual 'cast_acts' complication, and a WIS or INT search at medium is taken slowly and found (twice its minutes); about 60% of those 112 would have settled; hard checks, a check at someone, a secret, a body's risk, a danger stay rolls. (2) The prose killing a foe the dice left standing (rounds 10, 11, 13) is a conflict the guard retells with the foe still up; on its last telling the state follows the prose. (3) A foe the prose drives off (gone, fled) leaves the fight (G1: a routed swarm, refused, followed the player upstairs and bit her dead during a rest); and no rest while something fights you. (4) Taking a job is a lever, Take the job, listing the jobs heard of, offered or turned down and one in the deck whose giver is here (content: `giver` on seven hooks), not a row the referee may miss (rounds 11, 13, 14, 17). (5) Hiding takes a DEX check, and Hidden ends when the player moves on (round 16: 'slip down into the Barrow' hid a barbarian for three places). (6) A purse taken is opened, 1d6+1 gold (round 12's purses stayed items). (7) Only the hostile may be set on the player: a wary person's attack is refused until the referee turns them hostile (round 15's Gorm). (8) Second Wind is instant, like Rage (G1: it gave the swarm a free bite). Open from G1: the turn after a backstop death still adjudicated a check (a STR roll at 0 hit points) and the death was told twice; Gold +2 in the cellar before Maud paid; a second brass key never reached the pockets; the rats quest read '[done]; [failed]'.

Found and built: (1) Maud put the rats job before its reach fired and the referee opened 'Trouble in the cellar'; the deck's 'Rats in the Cellar' came later and the job was listed twice (a dealt hook whose giver is here is a quest in motion, so the referee's thread merges into it, and the merge brings it in as offered); (2) a light crossbow held with a shield on (two-handed weapons and bows take the shield off, a shield puts them away; gear.TWO_HANDED); (3) the whistle given back to Gorm came back to the pockets when the job paid (content: the rats job's reward no longer hands over an existing thing; the whistle moves by the story); (4) the turn tool showed a refused name as a bare traceback ('[REFUSED 400]' and the reason); and from Gemma pair 1, the turn after a death behind the prose takes no rolls. Open: a sword wound in the prose with no roll behind it (T30); Hazel's blow on the wrong zombie (T29); Tam 'down' on the sheet and climbing in the prose; a paragraph printed twice (T29); a mug of ale kept after drinking; the drowned and 'two zombies' both listed hostile; no WARNING on the night river (no hazard there for the narrator to call).

- B354 *built in part (Gemma pair 4, 11-11 early, 3f0270f2d99c, p19g-pair4: a Fighter in the Catacombs on the prerolled build; 21 turns, dead at T21).* The prerolled round held on Gemma too: the dice lines matched the prose on every fight turn (fight 1 blow by blow, the flee's parting blows told exactly), no turn retold, nothing dead acted. - B382 *built (11-13; cause found).* The box's llama-server dropped out under the probes on 11-12 night and looped on restart 20 times on 11-13. Cause, read in its journal: the gemma4-31b profile said `ctx: auto`, and llama.cpp's --fit gives up when the layers are pinned to the GPU ("n_gpu_layers already set by user to 999, abort"), so nothing was sized: 49,152 tokens a slot, 31.7 GB of the 5090's 32.6 with the weights. After an idle sleep the reload needs the whole card again, and any other use of it (a Sunshine session, 0.8 GB, on 11-13) fails the last 1.3 GB allocation; systemd restarts it, forever. Not the probe's load. The profile now sets 40,960 a slot (ctx 122880), sent to the box by tools/llm.py: loaded, 30.98 of 32.6 GB, 1.6 GB spare. The largest narrator prompt in the store is about 20,000 tokens. The other profiles that say `ctx: auto` (the card variants, qwen27b-auto) have the same fault and are not in use. *The first cause, found 11-13 afternoon, open and the owner's:* each outage begins with the GPU driver's own fault, read in the box's kernel log: `NVRM: Xid 8 ... RC watchdog: GPU is probably locked`, and llama-server aborts with "CUDA error: the launch timed out and was terminated" (three times in fifteen hours: 11-13 00:24, 02:02, 14:46 local, each during back-to-back structured calls). The 5090 also drives the desktop (Xorg, gnome-shell, firefox, a Sunshine stream). With the context fixed the unit now reloads in about 20 seconds where it had looped; the tools that play through the box wait and try again (`reading_probe`, `flee_probe`, `staged`). What would stop the fault is on the box: the desktop on the 3060, or a newer driver; not tried, not mine to change.

- B404 *open (the staged suite's first run, 11-13).* '"Yield!" I shout at him, and I swing my sword at his head.': Gemma read one attempt, the shout as a CHA check, and dropped the swing on 3 of 4 plays; the player's blow was never rolled (once the telling played it through a STR check). Not the Yield lever (it was never picked) and not the tight grammar (the probe's two shout-and-swing sentences keep their blow 8 of 8 with it and without): the word "Yield" in the player's mouth pulls the reading to a demand. The scenario `yield_shouted_with_a_blow` holds it; to read the reading's guidance on a demand made with a blow. *The owner, 11-13:* "The player probably shouldn't be able to do an attack and a charisma check intimidation thing at the same time anyway. At some point we should probably have a clarification step if the user is trying to do too much in a combat round." So the fault is not the dropped swing but that two acts were taken at all: a combat round is one act, and a turn that asks for more should be asked which (the reading's clarify kind exists; no rule uses it for this yet). A design item; the scenario's key changes with it.
- B405 *open, measured, the next work (the owner, 11-13: "prompt cache would be a big red flag for Gemma and at one point it should have been working").* It is not working for two of the three roles. Over Gemma play since 11-11 (turn 2 on): the assistant's prompt was processed from nothing on 127 of 414 calls (31%), the referee's on 133 of 481 (28%), the narrator's on 19 of 482 (4%), the summarizer's on 33 of 33; tokens processed fresh a call: assistant 7,084 on average, referee 3,779, narrator 3,272 (the server evaluates about 2,150 a second: 3.3 s, 1.8 s and 1.5 s a call). Three causes, read in round 31's prompts and the server's log: (1) the assistant's and the referee's system prompts hold lists that change (the people's ids, the pockets, the levers on offer, the quests' endings) about a third of the way in, with two thirds of fixed rules after them: a foe brought in or a fight begun changes the system text, and on Gemma (sliding-window attention: a slot can only go back to a checkpoint, and those stand at message starts) a change inside a message means the whole prompt again, not the part after it; (2) the quest check, a small call of its own, runs on the referee's slot every turn a job is open, and each of the two then finds the other's prompt in the slot; the summarizer does the same to the narrator every sixth turn; (3) the assistant's messages are a window of the last exchanges that slides by one every turn, so nothing after the system prompt is ever the same twice (5,000 tokens fresh on a good turn). Fixes, each to be measured on the reading probe and the keyed sets since the first is a reorder of model-facing text: the changing lists out of the system prompts into the turn's own message; the quest check asked as one more message on the referee's own conversation (or a fourth slot); the assistant's window sliding in steps as the narrator's does. Worth about 3 to 4 s of a 30 s Gemma turn; the reading's length is the larger half (D264). *Cause (2) built 11-13 (the owner: "this absolutely should never ever have happened ... the lesson was not properly integrated"):* a slot holds one kind of prompt. `Config.side_slot` (3) and `Engine._side_role`: the quest check, the beat check, an aside's question, the sheet pass and the summary run on the side slot, never on a turn role's; the profiles in use ask the server for four slots (30,720 tokens each on gemma4-31b, the same 122,880 in all); a role that asks for a slot the server lacks is logged as an error. `tests/test_profiles.py` fails if two roles share a slot or a side call skips the side role; `tools/round_counts.py` prints the cache lost by role for every round. Checked in play: six turns with a job open, the quest check on its own slot, the referee's cache held on every turn after (6,325 tokens reused, about 2,000 fresh). Causes (1) and (3) are open.
- B391 *no fault (round 31 T21; 11-13).* A parting blow of 6 killed a raging barbarian at 6 hit points. Read in combat.py: the note "Rage: a step lighter" is written only when the damage was lowered, so the 6 is after rage.
- B392 *open (round 31; 11-13).* The telling outran the state in the fights: "a dozen pairs" of rats where the engine had three, three thugs flanking where it had one Thug Leader, an ally (the huntress) who never acted. No foe was put down by the prose, and no retell was called (0 of 19). Cause, read at round 31 T16: the telling showed "three men in stained leather" before any fight, the referee filed one person (Thug Leader), and the blow at him brought in one body. The fix is at the filing (the referee's new-person row for a group the prose showed should carry the group), which is referee-facing text and wants its own measured change; not done.
