**Added 2026-11-09 after midnight (the side chat's read of part eight §5 and the readiness report; numbered from B217).**

- B217 *flagged (the side chat, 11-09; the §5 round 219a98e03aa9, Gemma): once the field is required, Gemma marks every check `aim: secret`, so the secret-holder floor is back on every parley and on fights.* 9 of 9 checks carried aim secret, the two stabs and a shout at Tam included; where the target holds an untold tell the floor raised the check to high: the gold at turn 3 (low, edge -2, became high: 11 against 16), both stabs at Mirren (medium became high, 16). On Sonnet 0 of 21 checks carried it. The field asks the model to classify its own attempt and the local model cannot; the witness should be the engine's: the floor fires only when the attempt is not a fight (no is_fight intent, stat not str or dex at a person in reach) and the stake's success was reworded for a leak or says "the piece comes" or "she says more", that is, when the assistant's own stake is about the secret. `aim` then becomes advisory, or goes. Part nine's first item; until it lands the fantasy on Gemma has the B63 disaster again. Part nine written 11-09 about 01:15: §1 B217 first and alone, §2 B225, §3 B219, §4 B220, §5 B226, §6 B45, §7 B227, §8 B222, §9 B223, §10 B224, §11 B228, §12 the Gemma round again with four counts; B221 waits on the owner, B218 left as is.
- B218 *to decide (the side chat, 11-09; the §5 round): `danger: none` on both stabs at Mirren.* Since part six the one struck answers through the narrator's ENEMY call or the backstop, so a failed stab needs no danger of its own; the field is for a failure that lands on the player's body by itself (a fall, a rotten floor). Lean: leave it, watch the next round; a rule line only if a fight shows a failure that hurt nobody when it should have.
- B219 *flagged (the side chat, 11-09; the §5 round turn 2): a retold turn writes no settled rows.* The fiat (a true story offered, with its why) was in the reading, the turn was retold for a leak, and the retelling wrote none; the count under B213 is one short for it. The settled rows belong to the reading, not the telling: write them once, before the narrator's call.
- B220 *flagged (the side chat, 11-09; the §5 round turn 4): a masked future reads as nonsense when the leaked term is a person's name.* "what they keep back is amused and more open to conversation" (Elowen's name, hidden until learned, replaced by the mask phrase). B210's mask was written for a thing or a fact; a name should mask to "she", "he" or "the person", or the future should be rewritten by the assistant on the retell.
- B221 *for the owner (the side chat, 11-09; the readiness report): XP stays at 0 until one of the player's own quests closes.* A troll killed, an extortion paid and Wren found gave nothing in two 40-turn rounds, since neither closed a hook. The design's open question (XP for invented quests) is this one: XP for a kill by the foe's weight, for a milestone the hook names, for a found person, or only at a close as now. The owner's call; the engine side is small once called.
- B222 *flagged (the side chat, 11-09; the readiness report, the Rogue): Sneak Attack never showed and Rage is invisible.* Hiding rolled and the prose said "unseen", but no hidden state reached the strip and every attack was a plain STR check; the finesse sword never used DEX. Rage was used twice and nothing on screen said so (the Character panel's Raging chip did not show; why is unread). Read the ledger's flag rows for `hidden` and `raging` before fixing.
- B223 *flagged (the side chat, 11-09; the readiness report): money drift.* A copper cost a gold (Rogue turn 1; B69's shape again), a long rest added a gold (Barbarian turn 22), a two-gold pay landed twice. A guard on the referee's money rows: a copper price never touches gold; a rest never adds money; a pay the journal already holds is a duplicate.
- B224 *flagged (the side chat, 11-09; the readiness report): the People panel and the Journal run ahead of or behind the prose.* Names before the prose shows them (Gorm, Mirren); attitudes lagging the fight (Gorm hostile after his whistle came back, Mirren neutral while drowning the player); stale "last seen" (B70 narrowed seen_at and this is what remains); a journal line cut mid-sentence, entries the prose never showed, the Rats job "offered" after it was paid. Read the panel rows against the prose in one round before fixing; the "names before the prose" half is the leak guard's job on the panel.
- B225 *flagged (the side chat, 11-09; both rounds): the "it fails" branch of the words-decide read is never used.* Settled fails: 0 of 16 on Sonnet, 0 of 9 on Gemma. Plainly impossible demands still rolled (50 gold from Maud, Sael's tower, Maud's inn deed); on Sonnet one came out "very high, edge -4", which is the fail written as a roll. Either the guidance needs the fail named harder (the examples are there; the models still prefer a roll), or an engine rule: very_high with an edge of -3 or worse is settled fails, no roll.
- B226 *flagged (the side chat, 11-09; the readiness report, the Rogue): the siren's grab followed the player off the island twice* (smoke pellets; boarding the boat): a join that never left, the backstop rolling her each turn. `present_enemies` tests the same spot, so what followed is the hold or the peril, not the fighter list; read `land` and the peril's carry before fixing: a move out of reach ends a hold and its peril.
- B227 *flagged (the side chat, 11-09; the readiness report, the Rogue turns 33 and 34): heat stayed "nobody" after a robbery and a stabbing in open view.* Heat is the Watch's interest; a crime seen by people should move it by the referee's row or an engine rule on a `seen` flag. Read what the referee wrote for those turns first.
- B228 *small (the side chat, 11-09; the readiness report): the clock in the prose ("the clock has crept to 22:04"), "What does Wren do?" written inside the prose, and "[ERROR] only a typed turn can be retold" shown to the player (Rogue turn 28).* The first two are the narrator's and the guard's (B5's formula, the THIS TURN question echoed); the third is a UI path that reached a redo on a command turn.
- B45 *widened (the side chat, 11-09): a setup check.* The Rogue round's player was named Wren, like the ferryman's daughter, and the ending read "Wren died". The sheet pass or the lobby refuses a player name that is a cast member's first name in the chosen scenario (a plain line: "that name belongs to someone in the city; pick another").

**Added 2026-11-09, part nine (the side chat's read of part eight §5 and the readiness report; built in the build chat). The B224 lines are the panel-and-journal read of the Barbarian round (5528fb9033ac) by a subagent; turns are the snapshot turns, [uX] an undone branch the player saw.**

- B258 *built: a move names one thing; a reward does not take back what was given on.* Lilith's save 25a51df64905 turn 28: the referee's "move wooden whistle to wren" moved the brass whistle still listed in her pockets (matched loosely on "whistle"), so Wren held the brass one and Tam kept the wooden. A move now needs every word of the shorter name to match (strict). The cellar's reward (Gorm's brass whistle) is not taken from someone it was given to in the story. Her save corrected by hand (owner_fix rows): the brass whistle with Tansy, Wren's wooden one with Tam, as the owner said (turn 28's prose has Tam press it back into Wren's hand).
- B259 *built: a quest the rows say is over is asked about by name.* Lilith's cellar: the rats led off with Gorm's whistle, the referee's note "cellar quiet", no ending flag written, so the quest never closed (D165's f1L the same). The fantasy's quests_maybe_settled picks an open quest whose note the referee wrote this turn (or the log line names it) in ending words (DONE_WORDS, or two words of one of its endings' lines); the engine then asks the referee's model one question (quest_check_messages: the job's summary, the notes, its endings, the telling; why first, then the ending or none) and sets the flag it names; the quest closes and pays that turn. The reason stays in the ledger (quest_check rows): it may know hidden things. Checked on her real turns 9-11, Gemma and Sonnet: talk only (9) none from both; the rats led off (10, 11) cellar_quiet from both, after the cellar's catch-all ending was reworded (content: "the rats are gone and Maud has her cellar back, however it was done"). Her save itself was closed by hand as troll_settled (owner_fix row).
- B260 *built: a timer for the player's own plan never runs.* The grimdark save f083ca6fc352 turn 23: the referee had made "Ungdall heads down to seek out Maud" (who=player, midnight) a timer at turn 17; Maud came up instead; on turn 23 a night's sleep stopped at midnight on it while the prose said morning. A pending whose who is the player is never live (no clock stop, no firing), and a soon row for the player is refused.
- B257 *built: a turn cut off mid-stream leaves no half turn.* The grimdark save 2beb52d49390, 11-09: a page reload during a reroll of turn 17 dropped the stream after the player line and bulletin were written; nothing cleared them (only a failed step did, A2), so the next input would hold two player lines. Now Engine.play clears the unfinished turn's transcript rows when its stream is dropped (a turn_failed row, step cut off), and a turn that starts over leftovers (a server stopped mid-turn) clears them first.
- B256 *built: the assistant can close the scene to callers.* The owner, 11-09: "is there not just like a way that the assistant can reject a newcomer ... clearly this should not be happening in this location/situation"; "also like in a secret or hard to access location". The reading has open_to_callers (rule line and field, true by default; false only somewhere private or closed off, their own room with the door shut even awake and alone, a locked, secret or hard-to-reach spot, asleep, in bed with someone, hiding, a fight or standoff, an intimate or tense talk a stranger would plainly break up). False is a hard why for every hook arrival and happening with someone in it. Required in the local grammar (REQUIRED_BY_NAME), so Gemma cannot leave it at true. A fresh-eyes read moved the default first (the list first would lean a weak model to false). B255's word rule stays as the backstop. Not yet seen on a real Gemma turn: the owner rerolls turn 18 of 2beb52d49390.
- B255 *built: nobody walks into the player's own room.* The owner, 11-09, the grimdark save 2beb52d49390 turn 18: "pip just showed up in my damn room". Waiting till midnight in his room (door closed since turn 12), Pip's "at the player's elbow" came: the game knows only "the Drowned Lantern", and pip_tags_along asks no more. Now the readings say where (in_private, the last ten newest first): the player's own room, a closed door or bed is private; the common room, the stairs down, the street or leaving the room is not, nor a move anywhere else. While private, a hook's arrival and a happening with someone in it wait (a hard why: "the player is in their own room"). Also: "waits for someone to come up the stairs" is no longer the player on the move.
- B254 *built: a quest's deadline runs from when the player hears of it* (the owner, 11-09: "deadlines should be relative to being introduced"; found by part eleven's report: with word held far from the city, a quest could go by unheard). A hook keeps reached_abs, when its reach fired; its failed and done times and its steps count from there, and its own events not yet fired are set again from the content counted from there (Tam rows out alone two days after the player hears of Wren). Unheard, time cannot fail it; only its story (a flag) can. An older save's live hooks keep the deal's clock. Someone hostile at the inn still holds every arrival there (the owner: "oh well").
- B253 *built (part eleven §1f): the tests, and "by the fire" is no fight.* Through the pipeline: Tam's arrival falls due while the player crosses the room, is held (the hold row and the mechanics line say on the move) and comes on the next quiet turn. That test found the fight words reading "I sit by the fire" as a fight (fires? after the, a, by, his, her, my, their, our, your is the noun now), which would have held every arrival at a fireside. The other §1f cases are in B248-B251's tests.
- B252 *built (part eleven §1e): a hold is shown.* The mechanics row (the play page's detail and the save's mechanics) lists each hold as the ledger has it, "held: lamplighters_shadow_reach (fallback), far from word: Reed Island", with how long it has waited past an hour; the call report renders the ledger's hold rows. So a quest waiting is seen, not wondered about.
- B251 *built (part eleven §1d; closes B241): Hazel waits to be spoken to.* Lilith's turn 8: Hazel "has risen with her bow, plainly bored enough to follow anything", never met, while Lillian talked to Gorm and stepped into the channel. Content (notes/content, gitignored; reaches :8016 at its next update): Hazel's attitude 0; hazel_offers wants people.hazel.met (met alone: the engine has no said: predicate on the player's words, so the directive carries the condition: if the player has spoken of the wood, the Barrow or going somewhere dangerous she says the wood is no place to go without someone who knows it and waits to be asked; if not she only lifts her cup across the room). "She is bored" is gone. Lint: a happening with who whose directive says the person is bored, idle or eager, or whose person wants nothing in the file, is a problem.
- B250 *built (part eleven §1c): at most one newcomer a scene.* The grimdark save 3acb923619a3 turns 10-11: Tam walked up unmet, then Pip. Now a person the player has not met does not walk up (a hook's arrival naming them, a happening with them in it) while another person who walked up unmet in the last six turns (the ledger's happenings and trigger arrivals, and the met flips) is still here (Engine._newcomer). Met people (Tam back for his answer) are no newcomers; the newcomer never holds back their own later events. Soft: a hook held two game hours by it comes anyway.
- B249 *built (part eleven §1b; closes B247 and reopened B104): place before people for word from elsewhere.* Lilith's turn 28: the lamplighters' fallback at Reed Island at dawn, told as a reed-cutter poling past to shout a job across the water, because Sael (a siren, attitude -1) was present and counted as a carrier. Now a fallback never fires while the game's far_from_word says so (the fantasy: a place in WATER, WOOD or SOUTH, or a found place within one), whoever is present; it waits for the first quiet turn at a city place. The "nobody near to carry word" people test is gone from unquiet. The deck does not deal while far_from_word says so. A trigger reach keeps its where.
- B248 *built (part eleven §1a): one definition of quiet.* pipeline.quiet_why, used by word from elsewhere (B104) and by anyone walking up (B235), each why to the ledger's hold row. Hard: a fight, the game's own (peril, compulsion, someone hostile), a move. Soft: someone came in the last two turns and twenty minutes; mid-scene with a person here (this turn's attempt at them, last turn's check at them, a question waiting, last turn's telling quoting someone the referee had present). A hook held two game hours by a soft why comes anyway (Event.held_since, EffectContext.patience), so a busy inn cannot starve a quest; a hard why still holds it. Who is here: the game's people_here hook (the fantasy's), else last turn's present list.
- B235 *built: nobody walks up to the player while they cross the room, or right after someone else did.* The owner, 11-09, the grimdark save 3acb923619a3: "I'm just trying to walk across the room and I've been interrupted by Tam Reedy and then by Pip". Turn 10, walking from the bath to the stairs: Tam's arrival (the ferryman hook's reach, due 60 minutes after the deal, in the inn) and Hazel's "where are you off to" in the same turn; turn 11, on the stairs: Pip's "where are you going". The fight hold (B104) never covered a hook's arrival in its own scene. Now (engine, every game): a hook's person (in its own scene too) and a happening that brings someone (`who`) wait while the turn is a move (ON_THE_MOVE on the reading, or a move: transition) and for two turns after a hook's person or a happening's person came (busy_for_callers); at most one person comes in a turn. Bells, fog and the like still happen. The hold shows in the ledger's holds with why. Not settled: a hook could wait long if the player is always walking; its fallback still finds them anywhere later.
- B234 *built: a looping local call no longer fails the turn the same way twice.* The grimdark save 3acb923619a3 turn 7 (Gemma, :8013): "adjudicator failed: structured extraction truncated at max_tokens=1536". Gemma wrote one intent, then '{"action": "shout to Maud", ...}' about forty times until the budget ran out; the engine's retry was the same call at temperature 0 and looped the same way. Now the local grammar holds intents and fiat to four (LIST_CAP; the rules say at most three), a truncated call is sent again with the loop penalty (presence_penalty, before only after thinking), and a repeated intent or settling is kept once (without_repeats). The referee's truncations (the station, 11-04) get the penalty retry too; its rows are not capped.
- B233 *built: a wait meant for someone else is not asked "until when".* Lilith's save 25a51df64905 turn 27, 'I give a gesture that says "she'll go when she's ready, for now, wait"': the assistant settled the gesture to Tam (works), then the until-when pre-check (D149) saw "wait" in her quoted words and turned the turn into a question. The narrator told the gesture anyway, but a question turn records nothing, so Tam's agreement was lost; her answer "until something happens" then ran the clock 588 minutes. Now quoted words are not the player's own (own_words), a wait told, signalled or asked of someone is theirs (TOLD_TO_WAIT), and a turn the assistant read an attempt in (a check or a settling) is never asked. Still open: on a question turn the narrator may tell an action anyway (it was told "nothing happens"); now only bare waits are asked, so there is little left for it to tell. :8016 has it at its next update.
- B232 *built (part ten §1, 4666dd6): TA_DEV_MODE=off (server.dev_mode): no dev switch, X-Dev ignored quietly, hidden games never imported; api-lilith and lilith_update.sh carry it; docs/SERVERS.md is the servers' contract.* :8016 is not moved yet: it takes the flag at its next update, on the owner's go (the owner, 11-09: "do not restart lilith's server until I give you the go ahead"). Today :8016 lists no hidden game even with X-Dev (its worktree has none), but its Settings page still shows the switch.
- B231 *built (part ten §2, 935e3a2): Super Grimdark Fantasy (super_grimdark_fantasy), derived from the fantasy: its content root is the fantasy's, its own are its saves, title and mods; the old copy's content deleted (no saves of it existed); a lint test holds a derived game's root to another game's.* Shown with X-Dev on :8013 and :8014; never on :8016.
- B229 *built (part ten §5, b6244bb): an attempt that meets an open tell's condition is a fair trade, no floor (floor.skipped on the check and the mechanics row); the fantasy's first example said plainer.* Was *flagged (the side chat, 11-09, from part nine §12; 6735ec72e948 turn 2): the floor's witness catches a fair trade.* A true story offered to Mirren, who asked for one, was staked with the gain "the piece comes" (the rule's own wording for any tell), so B217's witness (b) raised it from low to high. The gain is a tell, but the attempt is the tell's own condition being met (Mirren's `when: offers something`), not prying. Fix: when the attempt meets a tell's condition for the person (the engine already evaluates the condition in `holds_back`), there is no floor, and the assistant's read should have settled it works (the fantasy's own first example). On Sonnet the example lands and the trade is fiat; on Gemma it was a check. Not blocking her game on Sonnet; part ten's first item.

- B217 *built (part nine §1, 3fffe54); the §12 counts to come.* The secret-holder floor fires on an engine witness, never on the model's aim: no fight, and the stake about the secret (masked for a leak, or "the piece comes" / "says more"). aim is advisory and left REQUIRED_BY_NAME. - B225 *built (§2, 6062202).* The words-decide text names the demands nobody would grant as fails; very_high with an edge of -3 or worse is settled fails before the call (a settled row, a hopeless precheck row).

- B220 *built (§4, 08d6a23).* A leaked person's name masks to she, he or the person by the record (a possessive to her or his); a place to "that place"; a fact keeps "what they keep back".

- B222 *built (§8, a2ee5ce).* Read first: the hidden flag landed (turns 5 and 13); the turn-6 strike from hiding carried Sneak Attack as a 0 line, unnamed, and missed on STR; the raging flag was on from turn 4, its chip in the Character list. Now: finesse uses the better of STR and DEX; the § line names Sneak Attack; Raging, Hidden and a peril are on the strip.
- B223 *built (§9, 76780f0).* Read first: "a copper cost a gold" was change-making (no coppers; one gold broken) and is kept, the strip now showing the coppers; "a rest added a gold" was five coins Tam laid on the table landing as one gold on a night's sleep; the duplicate was a reward credit that waited for the giver beside the player. Now: a night's rest or the Rest command adds no money; a thing is money only by its name ("a gold amulet" is a thing; "five gold coins" are five); the same sum within the credit window is the same pay wherever the giver is.
- B228 *built (§11, f3ef452).* The clock's time and "what does X do?" in the prose are engine rulings (a retell), and the game's lines no longer ask for the question; a clicked command's turn is never retold, and the page does not offer it.
- B218 *as it is (part nine): the backstop answers a failed stab.* B221 *waits on the owner (XP).*
- B224 *read (part nine §10), the plain three built; the rest open.* The People panel and the Journal against the prose. **Behind half of it:** `tools/turn.py` (and `autoplay.py`) kept a turn's first `done` event, so on a turn the guard retold, the transcript and the player's panel showed the first telling while the server kept the retold one (live s20 and s22; undone s12, s16, s17, s20, s23). *Fixed with §10: the last done is kept.* Built: a person's card is titled by role ("not yet named") until the telling, the player or the journal names them (names seen in the prose land in `names_heard`); a journal line is a whole sentence or is refused. Not built (not plain):
  - B224.1-7 *partly met by the name rule.* Names on the panel before the prose: Gorm (s5, heard_of from his condition rows), Mirren (s13 [u292], the described newcomer resolved to the scripted person), Elowen (s15, never told her name), Sael (s18), Mirren and Elowen met by the scripted landing's effects (s19, outside the applied list), Pip Tallow and Tansy Fell (s16, full names by `apply_present`), Oswin Pell (s14, the surname). Cause: any row on a person sets heard_of or met through `_know`, and nothing asked whether the name was said.
  - B224.8-12 *open.* Attitudes against the prose: Gorm hostile after the whistle and dead off-screen yet listed alive; Elowen wary, Mirren and Elowen neutral and friendly while drowning the player; Sael flipping wary to hostile; Tam neutral after his daughter was found. Cause: attitude moves only on referee rows (ENEMY calls and deaths never touch it); a retold turn's first-telling rows vanish, so attitudes revert a turn later.
  - B224.13-16 *open.* "Last seen" against the prose: Gorm at the Old Channel (content's seed), Mirren at Reed Island in the drowned court, Tam and Hazel at the Reedway on open lake, Tam at Reed Island after rowing home. Cause: `seen_at` is seeded from content and moves only when the person is met and on the player's spot after the turn.
  - B224.17-21 *17 built; 18-20 were the first-telling journal (turn.py); 21 open.* A line cut at 200 characters ("where the lord"); three lines from the retold tellings the player never read; a line naming "the player... himself" for Kragga, a woman.
  - B224.22-24 *open.* Quests: the Rats job "offered" to the end although Kragga went down and Maud paid 50 gold as a plain gold row (a payment outside the hook never settles it; the cellar under the Lantern is where it was offered, so the place rule never took it up); the Ferryman's Daughter "taken up" and live after Wren was found (`flags.wren_found`; nothing settles a hook on its goal flag); Hazel's thread "from Hazel Oakhew" though Kragga hired her. "A hook's standing follows its reward row" holds already (a paid reward closes it); what failed is that no reward row came.

- B213 *built (part eight §1, 4d0d02e); the §5 round's counts to come.* The local model never wrote part seven's fields: on Gemma (455f5604a798, 8c106d354426) none of 25 readings had edge, edge_why, aim, settled or why, so every check ran at edge 0 and every fiat was a "works" with no why. The llama.cpp `inline_schema` required list and object fields only. It now requires those five by name (`REQUIRED_BY_NAME`); the referee's row fields stay optional. Checked on one Gemma turn (a15cce0b8ce6, forked from 455f5604a798 at turn 4, the bone charm): edge +2 with its why arrived. Found with it: Gemma set **aim secret** on the gift (its gain is a tell), so the secret-holder floor raised a low check to high. **§5 counts** (219a98e03aa9, Pell the Rogue at Reed Island, twelve turns on Gemma, 52ff6c5; transcript and call report `notes/playtests/2026-11-08-fantasy-p8-rogue-reed.{md,html}`): **checks with a non-zero edge 8 of 9** (the gold -2, the crude joke -1, the charm +2, the bluff -2, the stab while she looked away +2); **settled rows with a why 2** (turns 1 and 9; turn 2's fiat, a true story offered, had its why in the reading but no settled row: the turn was retold for a leak and the retelling wrote none); **settled fails 0** (the gold and the crude joke were read open, as checks with a negative edge, not as fails). Beside the counts: **aim secret on 9 of 9 checks**, the two stabs included, so the floor raised the gold from low to high and both stabs from medium to high; **danger none** on both stabs at Mirren.
- B214 *built (part eight §2, bd8a5c7).* A surrender was read as a chosen death (5528fb9033ac turn 17, seq 275 to 291): "stop fighting the hands... Enough. You win" matched LETHAL. The yielding phrases left the pattern, and the assistant's read is the second witness (no check and no fiat, or the death named in its summary).
- B215 *built (part eight §3, b2aeaba).* Three tellings ignored a death (5528fb9033ac turn 23); the third shipped a survival scene over the ending. On the forced pass of a death turn the engine writes the death (the prose to the moment, one line in the game's voice, the stone); the backstop rolls no one on the dead and stops at a killing blow. Before it, 48ee158 (the Barbarian round's own finding): one die for one way out, so the player's escape roll decides a peril, with no second countdown roll that turn (turn 22's hack-free success beside a natural-1 drowning roll); a blow at what holds them is no giving up; a death shows its own § line.

**Added 2026-11-08 night (the owner's answers to the state page's "what waits on the owner"; the side chat, numbered from B200).**

- B205 *built (11-08 night, D178): zero hit points is death with no roll, no carry and no raise, and a lethal step kills outright with its cause on the stone; the rescue is B143, later (dd4be6c).* Was *ruled (the owner, 11-08 night): death is death.* "We just came up with a whole gravestone system; we can't have this 'wake up later' stuff. That needs to be explicitly not allowed except in the narrow case of saved by a party member or a nearby cleric, and honestly put that on the todo list since it might be complicated. Just death is death right now." So: zero hit points is death, the card, the line and the stone; the downed branch (D158's wake at half with a rotated cost) and the lethal CON roll at zero are removed; the carry to the Temple and the raise at a price (D157's `carried`, D158's Temple) come out of play and onto the list as a later item (the rescue exception: a devoted companion present, a cleric in the room), with the rotated costs as the raise's price when it returns. A lethal trap does not take the player to zero and "dying": it kills ("you should get a fun description of being lopped in half and a gravestone"). B204's dying peril is withdrawn in favour of this.
- B207 *built (11-08 night, D179): the words decide first; an attempt that plainly works or fails is settled as fiat with why, and only an open one is a check (5a82d9e).* Was *flagged (the owner, 11-08 night, playing Ungdall on the local model at Hard; instance 27dc328d28c5 T28).* "When the siren asks for something true from me, it makes literally zero difference what I offer, a crude joke or something soulful", and then: "this seems to be a problem with checks in general, it doesn't matter what the player says." The turn: Ungdall cut off half his beard and threw it to Mirren. The assistant read the words and staked a cha check at **low** (routine), with both futures written (success: "Mirren accepts the gesture as a true offering, granting access to her knowledge of Old Marrow"; failure: "finds the gesture absurd or insufficient and remains unmoved"); the narrator obeyed and called `<<CHECK cha low>>`; the engine rolled it at **high** against 18 (10 + 3 = 13, failure; the ledger's modifier line: "Mirren keeps something back: low raised to high", offset 4), and the narrator wrote the refusal after the die ("a piece of hair is a thing. It is not a story"). Two rules erased the assistant's read of the words, and a third emptied its stakes. (1) B63's floor in narration.py: a check aimed at a person holding an unmet tell is high at least; Mirren holds the Old Marrow tell, whose own condition is "offers something (a song, a story, a memory)", so the very act her tell asks for is forced to high. (2) The offsets stack: high 14, the game's +2, Hard's +2 = 18, so a "routine" offer is a one-in-four. (3) The stakes scrub (D115/D116, `scrub_hidden`): the success future named Old Marrow, a hidden term not yet learned, so the engine wiped all three futures and the check ran unstaked (`scrubbed: stake 1` in the call's row); the narrator then decided for itself what the failure meant. The narrator's word did not raise the difficulty here (`harder()` can, but did not). And nothing says the fiction decides first. **The lean, for the owner:** the fit of the player's words sets the outcome, the die only settles what the words leave open. When the words plainly give what the person wants (a tell's `when` met: a true story to Mirren), no roll: it works and the tell comes. When they plainly miss (gold, a joke to a grieving siren), no roll: it fails and the person says why. A roll only where the fit is uncertain, at the assistant's difficulty, and the difficulty *is* the fit (a close offer low, a half-offer medium, a stretch high). B63's floor applies to an attempt to *force* a tell out (a threat, a lie, a bribe for the secret), never to the act the tell's `when` names. The narrator's word never raises the assistant's difficulty (the assistant read the player's words; the narrator read the scene). The assistant's futures are required on every staked check; empty futures are a ledger issue and the referee hears of them. **The owner, wider (11-08 night):** "literally in any situation right now it doesn't matter what the player does. It doesn't matter if they have something to help or a good plan, good logic or bad. It just makes a pre-canned roll." Confirmed in the assistant's instructions: difficulty is an abstract scale (routine, a real chance to fail, hard); the only thing the player brings that moves a check is a +2 when their character blurb bears on it; nothing grades a check by the plan, the leverage, the thing in hand or the logic of the attempt; "talking to a friend needs none" is the only no-roll case named; and fiat, the one lever for "this just happens", is forbidden to change "what a person thinks or feels", so every social attempt must be a roll. With B63's floor, every roll aimed at anyone holding an untold piece is high at least. So the die decides and the words only pick the stat. The design to write: the words set the outcome first (it works, it fails, or it is open), the die only for open; difficulty graded by the fit of the plan, the leverage and the thing in hand; the blurb's +2 kept; fiat allowed to settle a social attempt the words plainly settle. Part seven, when the owner is ready.
- B208 *built (11-08 night, D179): `player.fighting` is the backstop's only source, joined by a strike either way or an `<<ENEMY>>` call, and THIS TURN lists the rest as could turn on the player (2669a19).* Was *ruled (the owner, 11-08 night): the backstop rolls only people who have engaged in combat.* Sael dragged Ungdall under from offstage, in the middle of a parley. She was not in the scene (not met, not with Ungdall), no blow had been struck; `present_enemies` in world.py counts anyone at the same place with a hold, grab or song move at attitude -1 as a hostile, so the backstop rolled her hold after the prose while Mirren was still bargaining, and the referee then wrote her into the scene. The owner: "not enough to be like hostile, they need to have engaged in combat, even if it's like an ambush the player is unaware of." So: being hostile, armed with a move, or at the same place never puts a person in the fight. A person is in the fight once combat has been joined with them: the player struck or was struck, or the narrator opened it with a call (`<<ENEMY>>`), an ambush included, where the player need not have seen it coming. From then on the backstop rolls them every turn until the fight ends; before it, never. Attitude and moves say what they do when a fight starts, not whether one has. A scripted beat may start a fight the same way, by the call. Not patience: no patience rule exists anywhere in the game (the only beat near it is Mirren's pending "lets the song free Tam in its own time if Ungdall offers nothing", about Tam). It is part four §4 as built: "each hostile present acts every turn", with Sael counted present and hostile by place, move and -1. The timing fits: part four landed at 15:45 and the merge at 15:50 reloaded the server; turn 27 (15:51) had no world row and turn 28 (15:55) did, on both tries of the turn. And both tries rolled the same hold (8 + 5 = 13): the world's dice are seeded by instance and turn (B85), not by the player's words, so a retold or undone turn cannot escape an enemy's move, whatever the player does instead. For the build chat with B207.
- B209 *to check (the owner, 11-08 night; lower priority, the owner's words).* The too_far ruling did not fire on some very explicit behaviour in the forks the owner played on the local model (Ungdall's branches). The forks' instances carry the turns; the owner will come back to it. Read those turns against the ruling's wording before changing anything.
- B210 *built (11-08 night, D179): a future naming a hidden word is reworded (masked as "what they keep back") and dropped only if the rewording still leaks; a tell's gain is written "the piece comes" (5a82d9e).* Was *flagged (the side chat, 11-08 night, from the same turn).* The stakes scrub drops a check's futures whole when one of them names a hidden term, instead of mending it. The assistant's success future for the beard was the tell's own gain ("her knowledge of Old Marrow"), the right stake in the wrong words; the engine threw away success, failure and complication together and the narrator ruled the outcome alone. The lean: a leaking future is reworded, not dropped: the engine masks the term ("her knowledge of [what she keeps back]") or asks the assistant once for the same futures without the hidden words; a stake is only dropped when the rewording fails too, and then the ledger says so and the referee hears it. The assistant's instructions say the gain of a tell is written as "the tell comes", never as its content. For the build chat with B207.
- B211 *built (11-08 night, D179): `edge` and `edge_why` on every check, one more modifier line (d20 -4 to +4; 2d6 -1 to +1, +2 for a plan that nearly settles it) (5a82d9e).* Was *ruled (the owner, 11-08 night): a situational modifier on every check, set by the assistant from the player's words.* "We need a general 'add a modifier' for every check. Not for levers, but for other types of checks it should matter: if you're slipping on a ledge but you have climbing gear instead of a free hand you should get a +whatever. Item stuff might be tied to inventory, but for convincing, clever ploys and so on we need situational bonuses and penalties. Needs a lot of prompting to give guidance." As built, the engine's modifier lines are all from the state: abilities, conditions, the wound ladder, renown on a social check, the target's attitude, a helper +1, the blurb's +2, armour in water. Nothing reads the plan. The design: the assistant's check proposal gains `edge` (an integer) and `edge_why` (a few words in the player's own terms: "the rope and the climbing irons", "she saw him pull Tam from the water", "a bluff with nothing behind it"), the engine shows it as one more modifier line ("the rope and the climbing irons +2") in the § line's detail, the row and the ledger, and the referee's view carries it. Range by die: on d20 -4 to +4 (a thing made for the job +2, the right thing and a sound plan +3 or +4, a ploy against someone who has reason to doubt -2, a plainly stupid attempt -3 or -4); on 2d6 the situational edge is the one modifier the discipline allows (DESIGN.md §7.1a), -1, 0 or +1, +2 only for a plan that nearly settles it. Guidance to write for the assistant, with examples per kind: climbing and physical (gear, footing, a free hand, weight carried), stealth (light, noise, cover, who is watching), persuasion and ploys (what the player offers, what the person wants, what they know of the player, whether the story holds together, what they have seen the player do), knowledge (a book or a person consulted, a thing in hand to compare), fights (position, surprise, a weapon suited to the foe, fighting in water or in the dark). Things in the pockets count when named or plainly in use; the assistant names the thing. The edge never replaces the difficulty: the difficulty is how hard the thing is for anyone, the edge is what this player brought to it this time. Levers, enemy moves and hazards take no edge (the owner: not for levers). Part of the B207 work, part seven. **The owner's shape (11-08 night):** "here is how your swimming checks with heavy armour happen; for conversations it should judge the strength of the player's words given the audience; and of course there are still the hardcoded modifiers from stats and such; the dice-based things make sense too." Part seven written 11-08 night: B207, B208, B210, B211, B63 narrowed, B99's note, B209 last.
- B212 *built (11-08 night, D179): a summary row is stamped with the turn it was written and `covers` the last it folds, the summarizer writes nothing after its folded turns, and `tools/restamp_summaries.py` restamps the old rows (634f5a9).* Was *flagged (the owner, 11-08 night, sent to the build chat; the side chat's diagnosis).* "The story so far includes stuff that no longer is true due to undos." Found in the record: the summary row is stamped with the last turn it folds (`turn = to_cover[-1]`), not the turn it was written at. In Ungdall's game (8e346753d427) the summary covering turns 13 to 18 was written at turn 31, after Mirren was killed at turn 27, and its text ends "Later events, now past: the skiff reached Reed Island, Ungdall insulted the sirens, and he killed Mirren with a sword thrust", taken from the state block the summarizer is shown ("what the game state already records, so you need not"). Undo marks rows by turn, so undoing past turn 27 leaves that row (turn 18) live with the kill in it; a branch copies live rows up to its turn by the same stamp, so both forks (f2c12e51c4a0 at @36, 27dc328d28c5 at @27) carry the kill in a story where Mirren is alive and bargaining. Two fixes, both needed: (1) the summary row carries the turn it was written at (its `turn`), with `covered` saying what it folds, so undo and branch drop it with the turns it knew about and the next turn folds again from the older summary (the folded turns are still in the transcript; one extra summarizer call after an undo); (2) the summarizer writes only the folded turns: no "later events", no "most recently" beyond them, the state block is for not repeating and never for foretelling; the guidance says so, and the ledger flags a summary that names a death or a place not in its folded turns. Check redo (fb5d406) against the same stamp.
- B206 *built (11-08 night, D178): `<<ENEMY id move>>` and `<<HAZARD name>>` rolled when the prose reaches them, a fighter the prose left out rolled after it, damage steps to lethal, and a stake's `danger` step for invented perils (dd4be6c, 748d653).* Was *ruled (the owner, 11-08 night): enemy and environment harm through the narrator's calls, like checks and levers.* "Enemy behaviour should be like checks and levers, worked into the narrative where even the narrator doesn't know the result until it happens." The design: a second inline verb beside CHECK, `<<ENEMY <person> <move>>>` (and `<<HAZARD <name>>>` for the environment), which the narrator writes at the moment the blow or the trap comes; the engine rolls the move against the player's armour class (or stakes the hazard's check against the player), applies the damage step or the status (a lever's shape: the roll, the row, the ledger), and hands the result back inline as a called check's result is; THIS TURN lists the hostiles present with their moves and the hazards in reach, and the rule "every hostile acts once a turn in a fight". Backstop, so a forgotten call still bites: a hostile that did not act in a fight turn is rolled by the engine after the prose, its blow lands in the state and the row, and the next turn's THIS TURN opens on it ("Mirren's hold landed as you turned: she has your legs"); no retell. **Damage steps** on every move and hazard: scrape (1 to 2), solid (3 to 5), serious (6 to 10), and **lethal**, the shorthand for "not survivable" (the owner: "treat lethal as 9999 damage"): hit points to zero and death at once, the cause line from the move's or hazard's `harm`. Different traps and dangers carry different steps in the content. **Invented perils:** when the narrator or the assistant meets a danger the content did not write (a rotten floor, a fall), the assistant's stakes name its step with one rule: lethal only when the fiction is plainly unsurvivable (the void, a blade through the body, a cave-in on top of them); the engine honours the step. The stakes' futures already carry the shape; this gives them a number. Practical without an overhaul: the CHECK call's parser, roll, inline result and ledger row are the pattern; the new verbs reuse them.
- B204 *superseded by B205 (D178: zero is death).* Was *for the owner (the side chat, 11-08 night; the owner's question "does the game do a game over at zero unless there's an immediate rescue? does it allow instadeath?").* As built (D157, D158): at 0 hit points the engine stakes a CON check against high; a critical or huge failure is death, otherwise **downed**: the player wakes hours later at half hit points with one rotated cost (a tithe, a thing left, a scar, a lost piece, a window closed, a witness), unless someone present and devoted carries them to the Temple first. So zero is not game over by itself: a plain failure or a success on that roll is a cushion the owner may not want. **Lean:** zero hit points opens the *dying* peril (B102): one or two turns of countdown in which a rescue can happen (the carry, an ally's potion, a healer present), the narrator told "{name} is dying; moments left"; the countdown's end is death; no waking hours later for free. The rotated costs move to the raise. **Instadeath:** today only a referee row naming an execution kills outright, and the narrator may not write one unprompted, so a trap, a fall into the void or a cave-in cannot kill except through hit points. Content gets `lethal: true` on a hazard's or a move's failure (a pit, a blade trap, a cave-in, the void: a check, and a failure is death, no hit points); the assistant's stakes may mark a check `lethal` only inside such a declared context, so a sword in the street never instakills; the chosen death (B100) is the player's own lethal context. With part four.
- B200 *built (11-08 night, D178): no modifier caps on any game (the old caps only on request for replays) and the five-step wound ladder, minor 0 to ruinous -5 (dd4be6c).* Was *ruled (the owner, 11-08 night): no modifier caps on any game; grave conditions may make checks near impossible.* "I think it's fair to have extenuating things that help or hurt a roll, as our recent experiences with the fantasy have shown. If Frank has a shattered back, that's basically game over: almost all physical checks should be virtually impossible due to the modifier." So D168's -2 cap on 2d6 comes off as the fantasy's did (B103): caps off everywhere. The dice note's discipline (DESIGN.md §7.1a) stays as guidance for ordinary modifiers: on 2d6 stats sit in -1..+3 and routine situational bonuses do not stack; a grave condition is not routine and carries the penalty the fiction says (a shattered back -4 or -5: medium at 8% or 3% on 2d6, which is the point). The wounds table (D157) gets a five-step ladder (the owner, 11-08 night): **minor 0** (a warning: a word in the row and the panel, no modifier), **moderate -1** (Frank's bad leg, on the rolls it bears on), **serious -2**, **grave -4**, and a named ruinous wound (the back, both legs, blinded) **-5** on the checks it bears on. The engine's weights grow a step (minor, moderate, serious, grave) and `derived_health` maps the four; the referee's words table gives each its examples.
- B201 *built (11-08 night, D178): noir's talk row and the rules' line are 5 to 15 minutes (0638909).* Was *ruled (the owner, 11-08 night): noir's talk row is 5 to 15 minutes* (was 5 to 20; the rules' own "10 to 20" changes to match). Closes B28.
- B202 *built (11-08 night, D178): the locked-out start keeps the cameras and the crew's chat channel, microphones the first project (0638909).* Was *ruled (the owner, 11-08 night): the locked-out station starts with the cameras and the crew's chat channel.* "The station can start with cameras, and any questions people talk to the AI chatbot with, like 'hey, the Earth comms are down'; but no microphone audio yet, so it can't spy on conversations (we cannot read lips)." So the opening's camera lines stand (B30 closed), the senses line says what the station sees and that it hears only what is said to it, microphones are the first project, and comms, drones and the auto-doc unlock with their projects (B32: what each unlocks is the content's to name per project; the lean is the obvious: comms the Earth link and the crew's messages, drones the hands, the auto-doc the bodies). B61 (how the sample can be learned) is still the content's.
- B203 *built (11-08 night, D178): B66's thing is "a frayed bell-rope, green with lake-weed" and the lint says a secret term is never a thing's name; B70's `seen_at` comes only from a sighting the prose shows, a person heard of shows "said to be at" (ca47f24).* Was *ruled (the owner, 11-08 night: "I don't know what you're waiting on"): nothing waits on the fantasy's content questions.* B64, B65, B68 and B69 landed (D175, 71744e4); the record's six questions were built to their leans. The two left are the build chat's: B66 (the thing named "a frayed bell-rope of Old Marrow" becomes "a frayed bell-rope, green with lake-weed", and the lint rule that a secret term is not a thing's name) and B70 (`seen_at` is set only by a sighting the prose shows, never by hearsay of where someone trades; a person heard of has a "said to be at" instead).

**Added 2026-11-08 midday (the lobby, the saved games and dev mode; the owner's asks, pulled to the front of the line;
the design is `notes/DESIGN-lobby-and-saves-2026-11-08.md`; the first slice of U2).**

**The owner's game of 11-08 evening (Marrowgate — Ungdall, 8e346753d427, Sonnet, 35 turns): "what a disaster ... this game needs to feel MUCH more deadly or it's no fun." Read by the side chat from the ledger.**

- B94 *built (11-08 evening, D176): the stakes live by default; every profile names `stakes: live`, the suite pins off, and the settings card shows a dial that is not live in red (653e6bc).* Was *found (the side chat, 11-08 evening): the fantasy's hosted profile runs with the stakes OFF.* `profiles/sonnet.yml` says `stakes: "off"` (inherited from config.yml's default), so the assistant writes no futures for any check: a failed attack has no staked "the enemy's blow lands", the lethal stake never arises, and the only path to a lost hit point is the narrator choosing to write a blow and the referee recording it. In 35 turns and ten checks the narrator never did: Ungdall is at 15 of 15 hit points after diving into a lake in mail, killing a siren, and being dragged by the legs for three turns. The D115 to D131 machinery that made noir's live rounds bite is switched off for her game. Fix: `stakes: live` on the hosted profile and on every fantasy profile; f1L and f2S also ran stakes off, so no fantasy round has yet been played with them.
- B95 *found (the side chat, 11-08 evening): the world never hits back by the dice.* Hit points are the referee's ("a blow the prose shows landing") and nothing in the engine makes a failed attack or a failed escape cost any; the consequence table for STR drew "It makes noise: anyone near enough knows someone was here" for a huge failure while sirens held his legs (T33). Wanted (the owner: "much more deadly"): in a fight, a hazard or an escape under threat, a failed check costs hit points from the dice, by the referee's own bounds (failure a scrape 1 to 2, huge failure a solid blow 3 to 5, critical a serious hit 6 to 10), applied by the engine and told to the narrator in THIS TURN as the outcome is, the prose then showing the blow; the fantasy's STR and DEX table in a fight draws blows first, noise and the law only outside one; a hostile pending that fires (p21 "the sirens close", p23 "drag") carries a blow when the player's check against it fails; a scene does not end because one check passed (T34: one high success and the sirens "did not follow"): the fight mark (D150) keeps the threat alive until the pacing rule's "something gives" is paid for. **And enemies act (the owner, 11-08 evening: "enemies shouldn't wait for you to fail, they should actively attack you"):** in a fight every hostile person present gets a move of their own each turn, staked by the engine as a defence check against the player (DEX to dodge, STR to hold, CON to take it) with the blow on a failure by the bounds above and the stakes' futures written for it; the player's own attempt is a second check in the same turn, not the only one. The sirens held Ungdall's legs for three turns and never once struck; under this rule each of those turns was a blow he had to beat.
- B96 *built (11-08 evening, D176): the conditions table `notes/content/fantasy/conditions.yml` (17 rows: name, stat, mod, words, duration, aliases, text; enthralled carries the `compelled` block, `by` filled from the row's note, not yet enforced), read by `games/fantasy/conditions.py`. The referee's rule lists the names ("Conditions are only these names: ..."); a name, an alias or either followed by more words lands with the table's stat, mod and duration; an injury is still a wound and a lost eye, hand or ear still LOST_PIECES'; any other phrase is a journal line ("Ungdall: clinging to the skiff's gunwale", whole, no 40-character cut) and a report line, never a condition; a phrase that starts with an ending ("drowned", "dead") is refused with no journal line, which the narrator would read as having happened. Durations are the engine's: `minutes` clears when the clock passes (an expiry minute on the condition, `after_turn`), a short or long rest clears until_warm/until_dry, a long rest until_rest; until_treated/until_freed only by `-name`; lasting never. The Marks panel shows "DEX -1, until dry". The lint checks the table. Unseen in a live round.* *found (the side chat, 11-08 evening): conditions are sentences with no mechanics.* The referee wrote "clinging to the skiff's gunwale, legs st", "gripped by sirens, being dragged over th", "marked legs: ringed with finger-marks, n" (cut at 40 characters, `mod: 0`, no stat, no duration) and "enthralled by the siren song" (cleared only by Mirren's death, never read by a rule). A condition is a short name with a stat, a modifier and a duration (soaked: DEX -1 until dry; chilled: CON -2 until warm; enthralled: see B97), from a table the game names, the referee picking the name and the engine supplying the mechanics; a sentence is a journal line, not a condition.
- B97 *built (11-08 evening, D176): before an attempt against the singer, away from them or against the pull, the engine rolls the resist check at the floor the condition names, and a failure means the attempt does not happen and nothing is called (748d653).* Was *asked (the owner, 11-08 evening): compulsions.* "If you're under the siren's song you should need a WIS check to do anything hostile to them or even to move away or stand still, from running (hard) to attacking (even harder)." A condition type `compelled {by, resist_stat, hold, leave, attack}` set by content (the song: by the sirens, WIS, medium to hold still, high to leave, very high to strike); the engine stakes the resist check itself before any such attempt (the assistant stakes nothing against the compeller below that floor) and a failed resist means the attempt does not happen and the pull wins the turn. Ungdall's T29 to T30 (push off, dive and stab) under the song had no such check.
- B98 *built (11-08 night, D176, D178): hazards per place in the city file, called by the narrator as `<<HAZARD name>>` with an armour penalty per step of armour worn, and rolled after the prose when the attempt walked into one with no call (dd4be6c, 748d653).* Was *found (the side chat, 11-08 evening): places have no hazards.* A dwarf in mail swam a cold lake for three turns on STR medium checks with no CON check and no cost. Content: a place may declare `hazards` (Lake Ambercold: cold water in armour, CON medium each turn in it, a scrape and "chilled" on a failure, a solid blow and going under on a huge failure); the engine stakes the hazard check every turn the player is in the place's condition (in the water, in the smoke), as it stakes the compulsion.
- B105 *built (11-08 evening, D177): the question pre-check (`engine/prechecks.py`), a trivial turn ticks no event or pending, and the Ask mode answers out of the story as an aside (e66235f).* Was *front of the line, full stop (the owner, 11-08 evening: a quick fix; ahead of part four's remaining work), asked: "I can't ask questions without time passing and stuff happening. If I say 'is the song still going' it just moves time forward, and in this case the song restarted."* The assistant has a `trivial` kind (0 minutes; it used it for "what does the child look like?" and "do they have legs?") but classes most questions as `action` with minutes, and any minutes tick the clock and its events (the song re-armed). Three parts (the owner's order: the pre-check first, "the same idea as 'is this the same quest'"): (0) **a pre-check before the assistant**: the engine matches the player's text for the shape of a question (a question mark; an opener such as is, are, does, do, can, what, where, who, how, why; no first-person action verb) and on a hit adds one line to the assistant's turn input: "engine note: this reads as a question about the scene, not an action; if so, kind trivial, 0 minutes, no check"; the assistant still decides, and a question put to a person stays talk. Logged in the ledger like the quest pre-check, so a round can be read for hits and misses. (1) **An Ask mode the engine owns** for certainty: a toggle or prefix beside the text box ("?" or an Ask button) that sends the player's words as a question to the game master: no clock, no events, no referee, no stakes; one short call with the state block and the last telling answers what the character would know ("The song is still going; it is coming from the west") or says the character cannot tell; the exchange shows in the chat log as an aside (a different style) and never enters the narrator's story context or the summaries; (2) **the assistant's rule tightened**: a question that is not addressed to a person in the scene is `trivial` (0 minutes, no event tick), as the schema already allows, with the two sightings as its examples; a question to a person is talk and takes its minutes, as now. The engine never ticks events on a `trivial` turn (check that the fast-turn floor, D146, does not force a minute on one).
- B104 *built (11-08 evening, D177): a fallback or timed reach waits for a quiet turn, each hold a ledger row (e66235f); the content half (a fallback names the carrier it needs) is the fantasy side chat's.* Was *found (the owner, 11-08 evening, "another fuckup"; the ledger): a quest's fallback reach fired in the middle of the siren standoff.* Turn 29, Ungdall waist-deep in a confrontation with Mirren on the lake, the narrator wrote "Word of the city reaches you in an odd way, a thing half remembered: a drinker at the Pike, last night, grumbling that three lamplighters ... It seems very far away." That was `lamplighters_shadow_reach`'s fallback (720 minutes after the deal: "Word reaches {name} wherever they are, by whoever is nearest ... a note in the pack if nobody is near"), fired on the clock with no regard for the scene, and the engine's "Quest: The Lamplighter's Shadow" line under the prose. Two rules: (1) **engine**: a fallback reach, and any deal or reach that is not the scene's own, waits for a quiet turn: no fight mark, no peril, no compulsion, no hostile present, and a place where word can arrive (people present, or the city; not open water or the wild with nobody near); the hold already built for one-hook-a-turn (B35) carries it, and the ledger shows the hold and why; (2) **content convention** (the fantasy side chat's README): "wherever they are ... a note in the pack if nobody is near" goes; a fallback names the carrier it needs (a drinker, a boatman, a market crier) and waits for one. B14's rule (a missed event must not force action) is the same family: the world does not wait, but it does not interrupt either.
- B102 *built (11-08 evening, D176): perils (drowning, freezing, burning, bleeding, falling, held) count down by the engine's own roll, a step worse each turn and uncapped, death at none left (ec1e2ea).* Was *asked (the owner, 11-08 evening): peril states.* "The second I started freezing in the water in heavy mail I should have been rolling extremely hard CON checks to not start seizing and drowning. Once I was being dragged in and failed to hold on, that should have been 'you're being dragged underwater, you have only moments of consciousness left, what do you do?' and if I didn't pull out some god-tier get-out-of-jail-free card I should die; even a check to kick off at that point should be at so many negative modifiers it's impossible to succeed without a nat 20." A **peril** is an engine state with a countdown and a stake: drowning (under water in armour: two turns of consciousness), freezing, burning, bleeding out, falling, held by something stronger. The engine opens it from a hazard (B98), a failed hold check or a hostile pending; each turn in it the engine stakes the hard check itself (CON to keep conscious, STR to break free) with the penalty growing a step a turn and no cap (B103), the narrator is told "{name} has moments left" and ends the turn on the question; a failure at the end of the countdown is death (the fork for a carrier), a success buys one more turn or the way out the player named. The pacing rule's "something gives" is the peril resolving, not the threat going quiet.
- B103 *superseded by B200 (11-08 night, D178): built first as caps per game (aa19a08, D176), then no caps on any game; enemies roll to hit against armour class (ec1e2ea), now through the narrator's `<<ENEMY>>` calls (B206).* Was *decided (the owner, 11-08 evening): no modifier caps on the fantasy, and enemies roll to hit.* "I'm realizing any kind of modifier floors/caps are a big mistake for this game": the d20 can carry stacked penalties (5% a point), and the cap is what makes a drowning dwarf in mail roll as if he were merely tired. The caps become per game: off for the fantasy (penalties and bonuses unbounded; the Hard-step offset stays), -2/+5 on the 2d6 games as D168 has them. **Enemies roll to hit:** "sometimes it might be a check (a STR check to hold onto the boat being dragged), sometimes they just roll to attack versus your AC / armour; either way damage or a status effect needs to happen, and actively." The fantasy gets a light armour class (10 + DEX modifier + armour: leather +1, mail +4, a shield +2) and hostile people a stat block in the content (`attack: +3, damage: solid blow`, a kind of harm); each turn of a fight the engine rolls every present hostile's attack against the player's AC and applies the blow or the status on a hit, by the referee's bounds, before the narrator writes; the player's own attempt is their check. This replaces B95's "defence check" wording where the enemy has a stat block; a grip, a current or a song is a check against the player instead.
- B101 *found (the side chat, 11-08 evening; the owner: "are stakes turned on for anything? what else has been turned off we spent all this time working on!?").* The audit of the dials: the stakes dial defaults to **off** in the config schema (`roles.py`/`config.py`, D109's "off by default"), and nothing since moved the default: the stakes are live only where `TA_STAKES=live` is in a launch entry (the rounds servers on :8013: `api-local-gemma-live`, `api-rounds-gemma-live`; so noir's and the station's live rounds, the parity rounds and today's smoke did run with them) or where a profile says so (none of the 38 profiles does; `sonnet.yml` says off). So the fantasy on :8014, the dev server on :8010 and any plain launch entry run with the stakes off. Everything else built since 11-05 is on everywhere: the checks called, the consequence roll (`consequences: map`), the guard (`rewrite` since today, `log` from 11-05 to 11-08, B78), the pacing rules, the hidden-term guard, the time rules, the dice caps and offsets, the wounds piece, the beats as engine rules. Fix: the default becomes `live` (shadow and off stay for the harness and tests, set explicitly), every profile names `stakes: live`, and the settings card shows the dial in red when it is not live on a game that has an assistant.
- B100 *built (11-08 evening, D176; amended 11-08 night, D178): a chosen death lands, from the player's words or an attempt that is no escape in a peril, and since B205 no carrier stands between (ec1e2ea, dd4be6c).* Was *found (the side chat, 11-08 evening; the owner: "I literally just drowned myself and it's having sirens drag me off somewhere").* Turn 35: asked by the narrator whether he meant to let go and let the water have him, the player said "both"; the assistant read it as surrender with no check and no lethal mark, the referee wrote the condition "dragged underwater by sirens", hit points stayed 15 of 15, and the narrator, forbidden by its prefix to write a death on its own, wrote the sirens carrying him off: a rescue by another name. Death is the engine's and the engine's only path to it is a critical failure at critical health on a staked check, so a deliberate lethal act by the player cannot kill. Rule (the player is canon about their own body, D133): when the player's words plainly choose death or an act that is plainly fatal (drowning in mail with no one holding him, stepping off the tower), the assistant marks the attempt `lethal` and the engine lands it: death, the death card and the grave, unless someone present and devoted carries them (Tam at attitude -1 does not). No roll stands between a player and the end they chose; the narrator writes it straight. *Turn 36, "drown" (the ledger):* the assistant read "player chooses to drown; no resistance", check null; the referee wrote the condition **"drowned"** and took the sword; hit points 15 of 15, no fate, no ending; the narrator wrote the death in full and the game went on. The state now says a living dwarf with the condition "drowned".
- B99 *found (11-08 evening, D176): her save is on Hard (the game's +2 and Hard's +2, carried from the copy it was moved from), not a double add; Normal is the game's +2 alone, tested (aa19a08).* Was *answered (the side chat, 11-08 night).* The game was on **Hard**: every Ungdall instance (8e346753d427 and its forks) carries `difficulty = hard`, so the sum was the game's +2 and Hard's +2, not a double add. Still a design question, folded into B207: at Hard a "routine" check on d20 is 6 + 4 = 10 before the B63 floor, and a high one 18. Was *to check (the side chat, 11-08 evening).* Every check in this game ran at offset 4 (medium 14, very high 21).

- B139 *(numbered B103 when written; renumbered at the 11-08 evening merge) flagged (11-08 evening, with B93).* The keyed sets' referee inputs move: a replayed turn whose words name a quest in motion now carries the [QUESTS THIS TURN MAY CONCERN] block, and the thread_open row asks for a `title`; re-grade before comparing a referee run across this commit.
- B138 *(numbered B102 when written; renumbered at the 11-08 evening merge) flagged (11-08 evening, with B93).* The pre-check fires on every turn a giver is named, and a city giver is often in the scene (Maud on every Lantern turn), so the referee is told "this turn may concern Rats in the Cellar" on turns about other things; a filler `set hook-….note` would then become the panel's line. Count the hint lines and the notes they bring in the next round.
- B137 *(numbered B101 when written; renumbered at the 11-08 evening merge) flagged (11-08 evening, with B93).* The backstop merges any thread the referee opens that names a hook's giver into that hook (the owner's rule), so a second, different job from the same person (Maud asking for something besides the rats) lands as the rats' note, not a row of its own. Watch the ledger's `merge` rows (their `why`) in the next fantasy round before narrowing it.
- B136 *(numbered B100 when written; renumbered at the 11-08 evening merge) flagged (11-08 evening, with B93).* The hooks' content has no player-safe line of what is wanted: `summary` holds the hidden truth (the troll), so the Quests row's line for a hook is the referee's latest note alone until each hook file gains a `standing:` line (what is wanted, by whom, for what; `{name}` filled), which the engine already reads. Content is the owner's.
- B93 *built (11-08 evening, D175): `engine/resolve.py` matches a turn's words against the quests in motion and hints the referee, lands a thread_open for a job in motion as that job's note (ledger `merge` row), and every game's quests and threads render as one row (title, line, standing, giver) with the hook's standing words; unseen in a live round.* *flagged (the owner, 11-08 evening, playing): the Quests panel shows one quest twice and in two shapes.* "Rats in the Cellar · heard of · Maud Tallow" (the hook's row: a title, a standing word, a giver, no description) beside "Maud's cellar rats: she offers two gold and the room for the week if Ungdall clears them; trap door behind the bar · open" (a thread the referee opened for the same job, against its rule "open one for a job ... that is not one of the quests in motion"; a description, no title, no giver). Fixes: (1) the engine merges: a new thread whose text names a dealt hook's giver or its title words becomes that hook's note, never a row of its own; (2) one row shape for both: title, a one-line description (what is wanted, by whom, for what), the standing word, the giver; a hook's description is its content's standing line plus the referee's latest note, an invented thread's title is the referee's `title` (asked for in the row) or the clause before its colon; (3) the standing words follow the hook: heard of → offered (its reach fired) → taken up / turned down → done / failed; "heard of" after Maud has named her terms is the wrong word. **The owner: "this is a pretty big engine failing."** It is the engine's, not the fantasy's: the engine keeps two quest records (a hook's thread, which it owns, and the referee's threads, which the model opens) and never resolves one against the other, while people (matched by surname, D-noir) and things (aliases and word overlap) already are; the referee's rule said not to open a duplicate and Sonnet did anyway, so the rule must be the engine's. **Before the referee (the owner's suggestion, 11-08 evening): a pre-check, no forcing.** The engine matches the player's text and the narrator's prose against the quests in motion (title words, the giver, the places) and, on a hit, adds one line to the referee's turn input: "this turn may concern Rats in the Cellar (Maud Tallow): note it rather than opening a new thread"; the referee still decides. The resolver below is the backstop when it opens one anyway. The general fix: a referee row that opens a thread, a person or a thing runs through one resolver against what exists (title words, giver, place, aliases) and lands as a note on the match or a new row only when nothing matches, in every game; the same family as noir's canister twice (R18, S12 (b)). With the fantasy's rollout, as an engine piece.
- B92 *built (D175, 7289e16: the twelve SRD classes as files, Rogue and Barbarian full).* Was *decided (the owner, 11-08 evening), with B91; the design is `notes/DESIGN-magic-2026-11-08.md` §7.* The twelve SRD classes replace warrior / rogue / mage, each with a progression file (hit die, casting stat, slots by level, the features a level as trait lines, counts or choices; the official progression jotted for the first five or six levels, no subclasses yet; Barbarians have Rage, a count a day and a flag while it holds). A class not yet worked on shows as "(under construction)" in the select and is still playable; the owner finds out which class the first player wants and that one is built full first. Starting kit is a setup select per class in the SRD's manner ("a greataxe, or two handaxes"), not a fixed sentence ("sword and board is lame"). A spell picker at creation for casting classes (the class's cantrips and 1st-level spells as cards, the counts the class gives) and at level up in the Level up panel (new features; spells to add and one to swap); every caster a "spells known" class in the first cut. The flavour of D&D, not the whole of it. **The first two, full, today (the owner, 11-08 evening): Rogue and Barbarian**, jotted in the design note §7a (Rogue: Expertise, Sneak Attack as a damage step, Cunning Action, Uncanny Dodge; Barbarian: Rage as a count and a scene flag, Reckless Attack, Danger Sense, Extra Attack; kits as selects; the level-4 improvement); neither casts before level 5, so the spell system (B91) is not on today's path. High priority, before the readiness rounds, so the rounds run the build she plays. *Trimmed (the owner, 11-08 evening; §7b):* Fast Movement, Reckless Attack and Extra Attack are out ("stick to narrative combat"; no feature that is a button to remember in a fight); features are passive modifiers, damage steps the engine applies, or flags: Rage (the Barbarian's whole point), the Rogue's auto-hide at 2. **A `player.hidden` flag, engine-tracked in every game:** set by a hide check, the auto-hide, smoke pellets or content; cleared by the engine on an attack, a loud act or talk (Sneak Attack lands first) and by the referee when the prose says seen; one state line for the models, a Hidden chip in the UI. **The level-up screen:** what is new at this level, the hit points, stat buttons at levels 4, 8 and 12 (click the one to raise), the class's choice cards, Confirm; spell choices land there later. *Also (the owner, 11-08 evening; §7c):* Strong arm and Backstab are out (the record's own traits, not the SRD's; the Barbarian has its +2 STR and Rage, the Rogue has Sneak Attack): a class gets the SRD's features in this engine's shapes and nothing homebrewed beside them. **Two hidden flags:** `player.hidden_from_others` (the player's) and `hidden_from_player` on a present person or thing (an ambusher, a pickpocket, a trap): the narrator sees them in a "present, unseen" block under its hidden-truth rule, the People panel does not list them, a notice check may be staked, the referee sets them seen with a quote, and the name in the prose before that is an early tell (B63's retell).
- B91 *asked (the owner, 11-08 evening), a design in `notes/DESIGN-magic-2026-11-08.md`, placement for the owner.* Magic as an engine system: spells as structured data from the D&D 5.1 SRD (CC-BY 4.0, attribution shipped; levels 0 to 3 first, about eighty), slots per spell level on the sheet from the full-caster table, casting as a lever (`Cast <spell> at <target>`) the engine validates before any model call (known, a slot free, a target present), the slot spent by the engine, the assistant staking the attempt with the spell's sixty-word card attached, the engine rolling the spell's dice as the referee's damage bound, conditions with durations as pendings, concentration a flag; a Spells panel with slot pips and a Cast button. **Context discipline (the owner):** no spell list in any prompt; a card enters THIS TURN only when the player's text matches a known spell (a compiled pattern over the names and the casting verbs, run before the assistant's call) or the lever is used, and an NPC caster's cards only while that person is present in a conflict; the call report shows every attachment. Noir and the station untouched. Four questions open in the note.
- B90 *built (11-08 evening, D175): the sheet pass, an engine piece (`engine/sheet.py`, GameBase `sheet_fields`/`sheet_text`/`sheet_phrase`, the `setup` writer in gamekit); one call in the assistant's slot at creation, rows quote the sheet or are dropped, a row no field holds lands as a note in the player's words, turn-0 `sheet_row` ledger rows "from the sheet", the Outfitted line under the opening, `Re-read my sheet` at turn 0 from the built state with the text box as the correction; the fantasy opts in; fake provider only so far (B120).* Was *asked (the owner, 11-08 evening, playing the fantasy): the sheet pass.* The player wrote "I have an axe" in the about text; the game gave a sword and later rewrote "I throw my axe" to "I throw my sword": the state won over the player's own sheet. - B85 *built (11-08, D172): each check seeded by its stat and difficulty.* Was *decided (the owner, 11-08 afternoon: "yes please add in that change"), to build with undo (B82).* The dice are seeded per game and turn (`rng_for(iid, turn, "calls")`), so after an undo the same attempt replays the same dice (H4) but a different attempt at that turn gets the same faces too, which H4 did not intend (D134 (b): a changed action that assesses a different check rerolls). Salt the called checks' seed with the check's stat and difficulty, and the consequence roll's seed likewise, so the same attempt replays, a reworded version of it still replays, and a different stat or difficulty rolls fresh. Fork keeps rolling fresh (a new id). Tests for all three. Line 2 of the death-card lines (B84) depends on this staying true.
